\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{3\pi}{16}\Big)^{j}\mathbf{A}_{N,j}\bigg(\frac{8^{0+1}\cdot 0!}{3^{0+1}\pi^{2\cdot 0}},\ldots,\frac{8^{N-j+1}(N-j)!}{3^{N-j+1}\pi^{2(N-j)}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{8}{3}\Big)^{N}\Big(\frac{8}{3\pi^{2}}\Big)^{N-j}\Big(\frac{3\pi}{16}\Big)^{j}\mathbf{A}_{N,j}\big(0!,\ldots,(N-j)!\big);

here the last inequality follows from the structure of
\mathbf{A}_{N,j}
, see (
3.9
), and the observation that the summation
constraints in (
3.9
) force
k_{0}\geq j
, while the succeeding identity is a consequence of (
3.10
).

Now an application of Lemma
3.3
concludes the proof.
∎

We continue with auxiliary estimates that will allow us to control the right-hand side in (
3.6
).
Our next objective is to bound from above sums of expressions resulting from the estimate of Lemma
3.5
.
Instead of estimating first each term of the sum, we choose another strategy leading to a more precise final bound.
The lemma below is essentially
[
40
, Chap. III, Sec. 3
\cdot
71, Form. (12)]
;
see also e.g.
[
32
, Sec. 4.1.7, Form. (19)]
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
N\geq 1
. Then

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{(2N-j-1)!}{(N-j)!(j-1)!}\,x^{j}=x^{N}\bigg[\sqrt{\frac{x}{\pi}}e^{x/2}K_{N-1/2}(x/2)\bigg],\qquad x>0,

where
K_{\nu}
is the modified Bessel function of the second kind (Macdonald function) of order
\nu
.

Standard asymptotics for
K_{\nu}
(cf.
[
40
, Chap. VII, Sec. 7
\cdot
23, Form. (1)]
) yield

K_{\nu}(x/2)=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{x}}e^{-x/2}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(x^{-1}\big)\Big),\qquad x\to\infty,

which shows that the expression in square brackets in Lemma
3.6
tends to
1
as
x\to\infty
.
To estimate that expression we shall use bounds for
K_{\nu}
from Corollary
2.18
.

In Corollary
2.18
letting
\nu=N-1/2
,
N\geq 1
, we obtain for
0<\epsilon\leq 2/3

(3.15)

\sqrt{\frac{x}{\pi}}e^{x/2}K_{N-1/2}(x/2)\leq(1+\epsilon)^{N-1},\qquad x\geq\frac{4(N-1/2)}{3\epsilon}.

After all the preparations we now come back to estimating from above

(3.16)

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)=(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)+(-D)^{N}\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}W_{t}(\varphi+2\pi n).

Here
(-D)^{N}W_{t}
can be interpreted as the main term, and the remaining part on the right-hand side as an error term.
We first treat the main term.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
N\geq 1
. Then for each
0<\epsilon\leq 2/3

\big|(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|<\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\bigg]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

Proof.

We consider
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
. Let
F=W_{1/4}
and
v=(4t)^{-1/2}
, so that
W_{t}(\varphi)=vF(v\varphi)
.
Using (
3.6
) and observing that
L^{j}F=(-2)^{j}F
we get

\big|(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|=W_{t}(\varphi)\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{-1}{2t}\Big)^{j}\Phi_{N,j}(\varphi)\bigg|\leq W_{t}(\varphi)\sum_{j=1}^{N}(2t)^{-j}\Phi_{N,j}(\varphi).

Denote the last sum by
\mathcal{S}
and let
x=9\pi^{3}/(128t)
. Applying Lemma
3.5
and then
Lemma
3.6
we obtain

\mathcal{S}<\Big(\frac{32}{9\pi^{2}}\Big)^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}x^{j}\frac{(2N-j-1)!}{(N-j)!(j-1)!}=t^{-N}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}\bigg[\sqrt{\frac{x}{\pi}}e^{x/2}K_{N-1/2}(x/2)\bigg].

The above expression in square brackets can be bounded by means of (
3.15
). This gives

\mathcal{S}<t^{-N}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}(1+\epsilon)^{N-1},\qquad x\geq\frac{4(N-1/2)}{3\epsilon},\quad 0<\epsilon\leq 2/3.

The conclusion follows.
∎

To estimate the error term in (
3.16
) we will need two more auxiliary technical results.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
j\geq 0
. Then

L^{j}\cosh z\leq\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2^{j}\Gamma(j+1/2)}e^{z},\qquad z\geq 0.

Proof.

We have

\cosh z=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{2n}}{(2n)!}.

Applying
L^{j}
and renumerating the series, we see that

L^{j}\cosh(z)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(2n+2j-1)\cdot\ldots\cdot(2n+1)}\frac{z^{2n}}{(2n)!},\qquad j\geq 1.

It follows that

L^{j}\cosh z\leq\frac{1}{(2j-1)!!}\cosh z,\qquad z\geq 0,\quad j\geq 1.

Since, cf.
[
30
, §5.4, Form. 5.4.2]
,
(2j-1)!!=2^{j}\Gamma(j+1/2)/\sqrt{\pi}
, this implies the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

The bound

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}e^{\pi n\varphi/t}n^{2j}\leq e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{2}{\pi}\Big)^{2j+1}\Gamma(j+1/2)t^{j+1/2}e^{-2\pi^{2}/t}

holds for
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
,
0<t\leq\pi^{2}/(4N)
and
1\leq j\leq N
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi,t
and
j
be as in the statement.
Observe that the term
n=1
is bounded above by
e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}
.
On the other hand, we have

e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}e^{\pi n\varphi/t}\leq e^{-2\pi^{2}/t}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/(4t)},\qquad n\geq 2.

The function
f_{j,t}\colon x\mapsto e^{-\pi^{2}x^{2}/(4t)}x^{2j}
is increasing on the interval
(0,2\sqrt{tj}/\pi)

and decreasing on
(2\sqrt{tj}/\pi,\infty)
. The constraints on
j
and
t
imply that
f_{j,t}
is decreasing on
(1,\infty)
.
This means that
f_{j,t}(n)\leq\int_{n-1}^{n}f_{j,t}
for all
n\geq 2
. Consequently, we get

\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/(4t)}n^{2j}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}x^{2}/(4t)}x^{2j}\,{\rm d}x=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{2}{\pi}\Big)^{2j+1}t^{j+1/2}\Gamma(j+1/2),

where to get the last identity we changed the variable
\pi^{2}x^{2}/(4t)\mapsto y
.

Altogether, the above gives the desired conclusion.
∎

We are now ready to estimate the error term in (
3.16
).

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
N\geq 2
. Then for each
0<\epsilon\leq 1/3
the bound

\bigg|(-D)^{N}\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}W_{t}(\varphi+2\pi n)\bigg|\leq\frac{21}{500}\,\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi)

holds for
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
and
0<t\leq 27\pi^{3}\epsilon/[512(N-1/2)]
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle(-D)^{N}\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}W_{t}(\varphi+2\pi n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-D)^{N}\big[W_{t}(\varphi-2\pi n)+W_{t}(\varphi+2\pi n)\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-D)^{N}\Big[2e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}W_{t}(\varphi)\cosh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}\sum_{k=0}^{N}\binom{N}{k}D^{N-k}W_{t}(\varphi)D_{\varphi}^{k}\Big[\cosh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big],

where in the last step we applied the Leibniz rule for
D
. It is enough we estimate suitably the expression

\mathtt{S}:=2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}\sum_{k=0}^{N}\binom{N}{k}\big|D^{N-k}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\,\bigg|D_{\varphi}^{k}\Big[\cosh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big]\bigg|.

To proceed, we distinguish the part of
\mathtt{S}
corresponding to
k=0
, call it
\mathtt{S}_{0}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
0<\epsilon\leq 1/3
and
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
and
0<t\leq 27\pi^{3}\epsilon/[512(N-1/2)]
.

Considering
\mathtt{S}_{0}
, we use the bound
\cosh z\leq e^{z}
,
z\geq 0
, to write

(3.17)

\mathtt{S}_{0}\leq 2\big|D^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n(n-1/2)/t}.

Since the constraint on
t
implies
t\leq\pi/8
, we can estimate the above series as

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n(n-1/2)/t}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-8\pi n(n-1/2)}<\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-4\pi n^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt[4]{2}+1}{\Gamma(3/4)}\sqrt[4]{\frac{\pi}{32}}-1\bigg)\approx 3.487342\cdot 10^{-6};

here the only equality follows from evaluation of
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-4\pi n^{2}}
that can be found e.g. in

[
44
, Sec. 5]
. On the other hand, to bound
|D^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)|
we use Lemma
3.7
. This gives

(3.18)

\mathtt{S}_{0}<7\cdot 10^{-6}\,\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

We pass to estimating
\mathtt{S}-\mathtt{S}_{0}
. Specifying (
3.6
) to
F=\cosh
and
v=\pi n/t
, we get

D^{k}_{\varphi}\Big[\cosh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big]=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{\pi n}{t}\Big)^{2j}L^{j}(\cosh)\Big(\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big)\Phi_{k,j}(\varphi).

Next, using Lemmas
3.5
and
3.8
we obtain

\bigg|D^{k}_{\varphi}\Big[\cosh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big]\bigg|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{\pi n}{t}\Big)^{2j}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2^{j}\Gamma(j+1/2)}e^{\pi n\varphi/t}\Big(\frac{8}{3}\Big)^{k}\Big(\frac{4}{3\pi^{2}}\Big)^{k-j}\Big(\frac{3\pi}{16}\Big)^{j}\frac{(2k-j-1)!}{(k-j)!(j-1)!}.

Combining this with the expression for
\mathtt{S}
and then applying Lemma
3.9
we see that

\displaystyle\mathtt{S}-\mathtt{S}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{N}\binom{N}{k}\big|D^{N-k}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{\pi n}{t}\Big)^{2j}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}e^{\pi n\varphi/t}}{2^{j}\Gamma(j+1/2)}\Big(\frac{8}{3}\Big)^{k}\Big(\frac{4}{3\pi^{2}}\Big)^{k-j}\Big(\frac{3\pi}{16}\Big)^{j}\frac{(2k-j-1)!}{(k-j)!(j-1)!}

\displaystyle\leq I_{1}+I_{2},

where

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=2\sqrt{\pi}e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\binom{N}{k}\big|D^{N-k}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\Big(\frac{32}{9\pi^{2}}\Big)^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigg(\frac{9\pi^{5}}{128t^{2}}\bigg)^{j}\frac{1}{\Gamma(j+1/2)}\frac{(2k-j-1)!}{(k-j)!(j-1)!},

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-2\pi^{2}/t}\sqrt{t}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\binom{N}{k}\big|D^{N-k}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\Big(\frac{32}{9\pi^{2}}\Big)^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigg(\frac{9\pi^{3}}{32t}\bigg)^{j}\frac{(2k-j-1)!}{(k-j)!(j-1)!}.

We will bound
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
separately, and first consider
I_{1}
.

Since
x^{j}e^{-x}\leq j^{j}e^{-j}
,
x>0
,
j\geq 1
, we have
e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t)}\leq j^{j}e^{-j}(4t/\pi^{2})^{j}
.
Combining this with Lemma
2.15
produces

e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t)}\leq\frac{e^{1/12}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big(\frac{4t}{\pi^{2}}\Big)^{j}\Gamma(j+1/2),\qquad j\geq 1.

Therefore,

(3.19)

I_{1}\leq\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t)}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\binom{N}{k}\big|D^{N-k}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\Big(\frac{32}{9\pi^{2}}\Big)^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigg(\frac{9\pi^{3}}{32t}\bigg)^{j}\frac{(2k-j-1)!}{(k-j)!(j-1)!}.

By Lemma
3.6
and (
3.15
)
we get for
1\leq k\leq N

(3.20)

\Big(\frac{32}{9\pi^{2}}\Big)^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigg(\frac{9\pi^{3}}{32t}\bigg)^{j}\frac{(2k-j-1)!}{(k-j)!(j-1)!}\leq\Big(\frac{\pi}{t}\Big)^{k}(1+\epsilon)^{k-1},\qquad t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{128(k-1/2)},

and from Lemma
3.7
we know that for
1\leq k\leq N-1

\big|D^{N-k}W_{t}({\varphi})\big|<\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N-k}(1+\epsilon)^{N-k-1}t^{-(N-k)}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-k-1/2)}.

Consequently, including the term corresponding to
k=N
,

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t)}\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi)\sum_{k=1}^{N}\binom{N}{k}4^{k}

\displaystyle=\Big(\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t)}(5^{N}-1)\Big)\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

Here we can bound the factor in parentheses

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t)}(5^{N}-1)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}e^{-128(N-1/2)/(27\pi\epsilon)}(5^{N}-1)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}e^{-128(N-1/2)/(9\pi)}(5^{N}-1),

and the last expression takes the maximal value over
N\geq 2
for
N=2
,
which is
24\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12-64/(3\pi)}\approx 0.041476
. Thus

(3.21)

I_{1}<\frac{415}{10000}\,\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

It remains to estimate
I_{2}
. Proceeding as in the case of
I_{1}
, we get

I_{2}\leq\bigg(\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-2\pi^{2}/t}\sqrt{t}\big(5^{N}-1\big)\bigg)\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

Here the factor in parentheses can be estimated by inserting the maximal admissible value of
t
,

\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-2\pi^{2}/t}\sqrt{t}\big(5^{N}-1\big)\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-1024(N-1/2)/(27\pi\epsilon)}\sqrt{\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}}\big(5^{N}-1\big).

The expression on the right-hand side above takes its maximal value over
0<\epsilon\leq 1/3
and
N\geq 2
for
\epsilon=1/3

and
N=2
, which is
3\sqrt{3}\pi e^{-512/(3\pi)}\approx 4.167091\cdot 10^{-23}
. Thus

(3.22)

I_{2}<10^{-22}\,\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

Collecting the bounds (
3.18
), (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) we conclude the estimate stated in the lemma.
∎

Summarizing the main results of Section
3.2
, Lemmas
3.7
and
3.10
imply the following.

Theorem 3.11
.

Let
N\geq 2
. Then for each
0<\epsilon\leq 1/3

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\Big(1+\frac{21}{500}\Big)\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

Note that
27\pi^{3}/512\approx 1.635097
.

3.3.
Improved upper bound for
(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)
when
t\lesssim 1/N^{2}

In this section we present another approach to estimating
(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)
, which results in a better
(in terms of the dependence on
N
) multiplicative constant in the upper bound for
(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)
.
This improvement, however, requires restricting the range of admissible
t
.

We start with several preparatory technical results. Define

(3.23)

h_{1}(\varphi)=\varphi,\qquad h_{j+1}(\varphi)=h_{j}^{\prime}(\varphi)\sin\varphi-(2j-1)h_{j}(\varphi)\cos\varphi,\qquad j\geq 1.

Further, for
\varphi\notin\pi\mathbb{Z}
let

g_{j}(\varphi)=\frac{h_{j}(\varphi)}{(\sin\varphi)^{2j-1}},\qquad j\geq 1.

Observe that
g_{j+1}(\varphi)=Dg_{j}(\varphi)
,
j\geq 1
, where
D
is defined in (
3.2
).
Thus
g_{j}=D^{j-1}\Psi
,
j\geq 1
, with
\Psi
defined in (
3.7
). We also introduce functions

w_{j}(\varphi)=\frac{h_{j}(\varphi)(\pi-\varphi)^{j-1}}{j!\varphi^{j}(\sin\varphi)^{j-1}},\qquad j\geq 1.

Lemma 3.12
.

Each of the functions
h_{j}(\varphi)
,
j\geq 1
, is non-negative and increasing for
\varphi\in[0,\pi]
.
Moreover, for
j\geq 1

(3.24)

h^{\prime}_{j+1}(\varphi)=j^{2}h_{j}(\varphi)\sin\varphi.

Proof.

Since
h_{j}(0)=0
,
j\geq 1
, and
h_{1}\geq 0
on
[0,\pi]
, it suffices to prove (
3.24
). This will be done by induction.

It is straightforward to verify (
3.24
) for
j=1
.
Assume now that (
3.24
) holds for some
j\geq 1
. By (
3.23
) and the inductive hypothesis

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{j+2}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\big[j^{2}h_{j}(\varphi)\sin^{2}\varphi-(2j+1)h_{j+1}(\varphi)\cos\varphi\big]^{\prime}

\displaystyle=2j^{2}h_{j}(\varphi)\sin\varphi\cos\varphi+j^{2}h_{j}^{\prime}(\varphi)\sin^{2}\varphi-(2j+1)h_{j+1}^{\prime}(\varphi)\cos\varphi+(2j+1)h_{j+1}(\varphi)\sin\varphi

\displaystyle=2j^{2}h_{j}(\varphi)\sin\varphi\cos\varphi+j^{2}h_{j}^{\prime}(\varphi)\sin^{2}\varphi-(2j+1)j^{2}h_{j}(\varphi)\sin\varphi\cos\varphi

\displaystyle\quad+(2j+1)h_{j}^{\prime}(\varphi)\sin^{2}\varphi-(4j^{2}-1)h_{j}(\varphi)\sin\varphi\cos\varphi

\displaystyle=-(j+1)^{2}(2j-1)h_{j}(\varphi)\sin\varphi\cos\varphi+(j+1)^{2}h_{j}^{\prime}(\varphi)\sin^{2}\varphi

\displaystyle=(j+1)^{2}h_{j+1}(\varphi)\sin\varphi.

The lemma follows.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

Let
j\geq 1
. The following two-step recurrence relations hold:

\displaystyle h_{j+2}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=-(2j+1)\cos\varphi\,h_{j+1}(\varphi)+j^{2}\sin^{2}\varphi\,h_{j}(\varphi),

\displaystyle g_{j+2}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\varphi}\Big[-(2j+1)\cos\varphi\,g_{j+1}(\varphi)+j^{2}g_{j}(\varphi)\Big],

\displaystyle w_{j+2}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2j+1}{j+2}\cot\varphi\,\frac{\pi-\varphi}{\varphi}\,w_{j+1}(\varphi)+\frac{j^{2}}{(j+1)(j+2)}\bigg(\frac{\pi-\varphi}{\varphi}\bigg)^{2}w_{j}(\varphi).

The relation for
h_{j}
is verified by combining (
3.23
) (with
j
replaced by
j+1
) with (
3.24
).
The remaining relations follow from that for
h_{j}
.

Lemma 3.14
.

The functions
g_{j}(\varphi)
,
j\geq 1
, are positive and increasing on
(0,\pi)
. Moreover,

(3.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}g_{j}(\varphi)&\leq g_{j}\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}\Big)=\begin{cases}\big[(j-2)!!\big]^{2}&\text{ if $j\geq 2$ is even}\\
{\pi}\big[(j-2)!!\big]^{2}/2&\text{ if $j\geq 2$ is odd}\\
{\pi}/{2}&\text{ if }j=1\end{cases},\qquad\varphi\in(0,\pi/2].\end{split}

Proof.

From Lemma
3.12
we see that
g_{j}
is positive. Further, using (
3.23
) we get

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{j}(\varphi)=\frac{h^{\prime}_{j}(\varphi)(\sin\varphi)^{2j-1}-(2j-1)h_{j}(\varphi)(\sin\varphi)^{2j-2}\cos\varphi}{(\sin\varphi)^{4j-2}}=\frac{h_{j+1}(\varphi)}{(\sin\varphi)^{2j}}.

This, in view of Lemma
3.12
, implies that
g_{j}
is increasing.

It remains to show (
3.25
). Observe that
g_{j}(\varphi)\leq g_{j}(\pi/2)=h_{j}(\pi/2)
.
Using (
3.23
) and (
3.24
) we get the recurrence

\displaystyle h_{j+1}(\pi/2)=h_{j}^{\prime}(\pi/2)=(j-1)^{2}h_{j-1}(\pi/2),\qquad j\geq 2.

Together with
h_{1}(\pi/2)=\pi/2
and
h_{2}(\pi/2)=1
, this leads to the formulas

h_{2j}(\pi/2)=\big[(2j-2)!!\big]^{2},\qquad h_{2j+1}(\pi/2)=\frac{\pi}{2}\big[(2j-1)!!\big]^{2},\qquad j\geq 1.

The proof is complete.
∎

Now we can estimate
w_{j}(\varphi)
, though the bound obtained is not optimal.
14
14

14

We believe that the stronger estimate
w_{j}(\varphi)\leq(\pi/2)^{j-1}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi/2]
, is true, but proving
this seems to be much more involved. In this connection, the relations from Remark
3.13
are potentially useful.

Corollary 3.15
.

For each
j\geq 1
,

w_{j}(\varphi)\leq\pi^{j-1},\qquad\varphi\in(0,\pi/2].

Proof.

Using Lemma
3.14
we infer that
w_{1}(\varphi)=1
and for
j\geq 2
and
\varphi\in(0,\pi/2]

w_{j}(\varphi)=\frac{g_{j}(\varphi)(\pi-\varphi)^{j-1}(\sin\varphi)^{j}}{j!\varphi^{j}}\leq\frac{\big[(j-2)!!\big]^{2}\pi^{j}}{2j!}\leq\frac{(j-1)!\pi^{j}}{2j!}\leq\frac{\pi^{j}}{4}\leq\pi^{j-1},

as desired.
∎

The next result is essentially known. It follows directly by combining an exact expression for partial Bell polynomials

\mathbf{B}_{N,n}
(see
[
6
, Thm. A, p. 134]
) with known particular values
\mathbf{B}_{N,n}(1!,2!,3!,\ldots)
,
see
[
6
, Form. [3h], p. 135]
.
15
15

15

The numbers appearing as coefficients on the right-hand side of the identity in Lemma
3.16

are the (unsigned) Lah numbers, cf.
[
6
, p. 156]
.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
N\geq 1
and
s>0
. Then

\sum_{k_{1}+\ldots+Nk_{N}=N}\frac{N!}{k_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{N}!}s^{k_{1}+\ldots+k_{N}}=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\binom{N}{n}\frac{(N-1)!}{(n-1)!}s^{n},

where the first summation is over all
k_{1},\ldots,k_{N}\geq 0
satisfying the indicated condition.

After the above preparations we are in a position to prove the following.

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
N\geq 1
. Then for each
\delta>0

\big|(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\leq e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\varphi}{2\sin\varphi}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we assume that
N,\delta,t,\varphi
are as in the statement.
By Faà di Bruno’s formula, cf.
[
6
, Chap. III, Sec. 3.4]
,
16
16

16

The classical Faà di Bruno’s formula pertains to successive derivatives of a composition of two functions,
see e.g.
[
16
,
8
]
for details and historical background. The formula has a combinatorial nature and
can be put into an abstract framework as in
[
6
, Sec. 3.4]
. Here we use a variant of the classical formula
with the standard differentiation replaced by
-D
, and the relevant fact that makes this replacement legitimate is
that
-D
obeys the Leibniz rule.

(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)=W_{t}(\varphi)\sum_{k_{1}+\ldots+Nk_{N}=N}\frac{N!}{k_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{N}!}(2t)^{-(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{N})}\prod_{j=1}^{N}\bigg(\frac{(-1)^{j+1}g_{j}(\varphi)}{j!}\bigg)^{k_{j}}.

Then making use of the functions
h_{j}
and
w_{j}
we see that

\displaystyle(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\varphi}{2t\sin\varphi}\Big)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{k_{1}+\ldots+Nk_{N}=N}\frac{N!}{k_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{N}!}\Big(\frac{2t}{\pi-\varphi}\Big)^{N-(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{N})}\prod_{j=1}^{N}\big[(-1)^{j+1}w_{j}(\varphi)\big]^{k_{j}}.

This can be written as

(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)=\Big(\frac{\varphi}{2t\sin\varphi}\Big)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big[1+E_{t,N}(\varphi)\big],

where

E_{t,N}(\varphi):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+\ldots+Nk_{N}=N\\
k_{1}\neq N\end{subarray}}\frac{N!}{k_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{N}!}\Big(\frac{2t}{\pi-\varphi}\Big)^{N-(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{N})}\prod_{j=1}^{N}\big[(-1)^{j+1}w_{j}(\varphi)\big]^{k_{j}}.

It is enough to prove that
|E_{t,N}(\varphi)|\leq e^{\delta}-1
and to this end we may assume that
N\geq 2
.

By Corollary
3.15
we have

\displaystyle|E_{t,N}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+\ldots+Nk_{N}=N\\
k_{1}\neq N\end{subarray}}\frac{N!}{k_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{N}!}\Big(\frac{2t}{\pi/2}\Big)^{N-(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{N})}\prod_{j=1}^{N}\pi^{jk_{j}-k_{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+\ldots+Nk_{N}=N\\
k_{1}\neq N\end{subarray}}\frac{N!}{k_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{N}!}(4t)^{N-(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+\ldots+Nk_{N}=N\\
k_{1}\neq N\end{subarray}}\frac{N!}{k_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{N}!}\Big(\frac{\delta}{N^{2}}\Big)^{N-(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{N})}.

Applying now Lemma
3.16
with
s=N^{2}/\delta
we obtain

|E_{t,N}(\varphi)|\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\binom{N}{n}\frac{(N-1)!}{(n-1)!}\Big(\frac{\delta}{N^{2}}\Big)^{N-n}.

Reversing the order of summation we arrive at

|E_{t,N}(\varphi)|\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\binom{N}{n}\frac{(N-1)!}{(N-n-1)!}\Big(\frac{\delta}{N^{2}}\Big)^{n}=\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\frac{N!}{N^{n}(N-n)!}\frac{(N-1)!}{N^{n}(N-n-1)!}\frac{\delta^{n}}{n!}.

Notice that the first two factors under the last sum are bounded above by
1
. Thus

|E_{t,N}(\varphi)|\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\frac{\delta^{n}}{n!}\leq e^{\delta}-1,

and this finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.18
.

Let
N\geq 1
. Then for each
\delta>0

\big|(-D)^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\leq e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}\,t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

Next, we estimate the error term in (
3.16
).

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
N\geq 1
. Then for each
0<\delta\leq N

\bigg|(-D)^{N}\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}W_{t}(\varphi+2\pi n)\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{1000}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

Proof.

The reasoning is parallel to the proof of Lemma
3.10
, the main difference is that now we use Corollary
3.18

instead of Lemma
3.7
. Adopting the notation from the proof of Lemma
3.10
, we need to bound
\mathtt{S}
,
and we split this task to estimating separately
\mathtt{S}_{0}
,
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
(these expressions are now the same as before).
Throughout the proof we assume that
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
,
\delta\leq N
and
t\leq\delta/(4N^{2})
.
Then
t
under consideration is not bigger than
1/(4N)
. Further, we specify
\epsilon=128/(27\pi^{3})
so that

1/(4N)<27\pi^{3}\epsilon/[512(N-1/2)]
.

Since the constraint on
t
implies
t\leq 1/4<\pi/10
, we have (see (
3.17
))

\mathtt{S}_{0}\leq 2\big|D^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}n(n-1/2)/t}\leq\big|D^{N}W_{t}(\varphi)\big|\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-5\pi n^{2}}.

Evaluating the last series
17
17

17

We could use more precise bound
1/4<\pi/12
and evaluate the series
\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-6\pi n^{2}}
with the
aid of
[
44
, Thm. 5.5(vi)]
, but the formula would be much more complicated.

by means of
[
44
, Thm. 5.5(v)]
we get

\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-5\pi n^{2}}=\frac{\pi^{1/4}\sqrt{5+2\sqrt{5}}}{\Gamma(3/4)5^{3/4}}-1\approx 3.014035\cdot 10^{-7}.

This, together with Corollary
3.18
, gives

\mathtt{S}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}10^{-6}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

Considering
I_{1}
, combining (
3.19
) with (
3.20
) and Corollary
3.18
produces

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}}{1+\epsilon}e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t)}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi)\sum_{k=1}^{N}\binom{N}{k}\Big[\frac{64}{3\pi}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\{\frac{\sqrt{2}\,e^{1/12}}{1+\epsilon}\bigg(\Big[\frac{64}{3\pi}(1+\epsilon)+1\Big]^{N}-1\bigg)e^{-\pi^{2}N}\bigg\}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi).

Recall that
\epsilon=128/(27\pi^{3})
. The expression in curly brackets above is maximal over
N=1,2,\ldots
for
N=1
, and the maximal
value is
\approx 0.000540
. Hence

I_{1}\leq 6\cdot 10^{-4}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

Estimation of
I_{2}
is quite similar, we get

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}(1+\epsilon)}\sqrt{t}e^{-2\pi^{2}/t}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi)\sum_{k=1}^{N}\binom{N}{k}\Big[\frac{64}{3\pi}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\{\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}(1+\epsilon)}\sqrt{\frac{1}{4N}}\bigg(\Big[\frac{64}{3\pi}(1+\epsilon)+1\Big]^{N}-1\bigg)e^{-8\pi^{2}N}\bigg\}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi).

Again the expression in curly brackets takes its maximal value for
N=1
, which is
\approx 1.962529\cdot 10^{-34}
. Consequently,

I_{2}\leq 2\cdot 10^{-34}e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

The lemma follows directly from the above bounds for
\mathtt{S}_{0}
,
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
.
∎

Summarizing the main results of Section
3.3
, Corollary
3.18
and Lemma
3.19
imply the following.

Theorem 3.20
.

Let
N\geq 1
. Then for each
0<\delta\leq N

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\Big(1+\frac{1}{1000}\Big)e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

Note that
e\pi/4\approx 2.134934
(this constant arises as exponentiation base with the choice
\delta=N
).

3.4.
Upper bounds for
\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)
and
-D\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)

We will use simple direct analysis of the series defining
\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)
to establish upper bounds for

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)
when
N=0,1
.
For
N=1
this will be a refinement of the previously obtained estimates.

Proposition 3.21
.

One has

\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\Big[1+2e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}\Big]W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\pi^{2}/2.

Proof.

Observe that

\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)=W_{t}(\varphi)\bigg(1+\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-\pi n(\varphi+\pi n)/t}\bigg).

Denote the above series by
S_{t}(\varphi)
. Assuming that
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
, we can write

\displaystyle S_{t}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi(\pi-\varphi)/t}+\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z},\,n\neq 0,-1}e^{-\pi n(\varphi+\pi n)/t}\leq e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}+\sum_{n\geq 1}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}+\sum_{n\leq-2}e^{-\pi^{2}n(2n+1)/(2t)}

\displaystyle<e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}+2\sum_{n\geq 1}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}<e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}+2\sum_{n\geq 1}e^{-\pi^{2}n/t}=e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}+\frac{2e^{-\pi^{2}/t}}{1-e^{-\pi^{2}/t}}.

Thus

\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<W_{t}(\varphi)\bigg[1+e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}+\frac{2e^{-\pi^{2}/t}}{1-e^{-\pi^{2}/t}}\bigg],\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t>0.

It is elementary to check that the second term in square brackets above dominates the third one when
t\leq\pi^{2}/2
.
The conclusion follows.
∎

Note that the expression in square brackets in Proposition
3.21
is increasing in
t
.
Its value at
t=1
is
\approx 1.014384
and at
t=\pi/2
the value is
\approx 1.086428
.

Proposition 3.22
.

One has

-D\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\frac{\pi}{4}t^{-1}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad 0<t\leq\pi^{2}/2.

Proof.

By a direct computation we see that

-D\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)=t^{-1}W_{t}(\varphi)\frac{\varphi}{2\sin\varphi}\bigg(1+\sum_{0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{\varphi}(\varphi+2\pi n)e^{-\pi n(\varphi+\pi n)/t}\bigg).

We need to estimate the above series, which we denote by
\mathcal{S}_{t}(\varphi)
.
To control the relevant cancellations we pair terms corresponding to
\pm n
. This leads to

\mathcal{S}_{t}(\varphi)=\sum_{n\geq 1}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}\Big[2\cosh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}-\frac{4\pi n}{\varphi}\sinh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big],

where the value at
\varphi=0
of each term is understood in the limiting sense.

Denote the expression in square brackets above by
f_{n,t}(\varphi)
. We claim that
\varphi\mapsto f_{n,t}(\varphi)

is decreasing on
[0,\pi/2]
for each fixed
n\geq 1
and
0<t\leq\pi^{2}n^{2}/2
. To prove the claim, we compute

f^{\prime}_{n,t}(\varphi)=\frac{2\pi n}{\varphi t}\Big[\Big(\varphi+\frac{2t}{\varphi}\Big)\sinh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}-2\pi n\cosh\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}\Big]

and observe that it is enough to show

(3.26)

\frac{\varphi}{2\pi n}\leq\coth\frac{\pi n\varphi}{t}-\frac{t}{\pi n\varphi},\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}n^{2}}{2},\quad n\geq 1.

Let
x=\pi n\varphi/t
. It is straightforward to check that

\frac{\varphi}{2\pi n}\leq\frac{1\wedge x}{4},\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}n^{2}}{2},\quad n\geq 1.

Then (
3.26
) follows from the bound
(1\wedge x)/4\leq\coth x-1/x
,
x>0
, which is elementary to verify.
The claim follows.

From the claim we infer that

f_{n,t}(\varphi)\leq f_{n,t}(0)=2-\frac{4\pi^{2}n^{2}}{t}<0,\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}n^{2}}{2},\quad n\geq 1.

This leads to

\mathcal{S}_{t}(\varphi)\leq\sum_{n\geq 1}e^{-\pi^{2}n^{2}/t}\bigg[2-\frac{4\pi^{2}n^{2}}{t}\bigg]<0,\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}.

Consequently,

-D\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<t^{-1}W_{t}(\varphi)\frac{\varphi}{2\sin\varphi}\leq\frac{\pi}{4}t^{-1}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{2},

as stated in the lemma.
∎

3.5.
Summary of bounds for
(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)

The result below is a compilation of the lower bound from Proposition
3.1
restricted to
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]

and Theorem
3.11
, Theorem
3.20
and Propositions
3.21
and
3.22
.

Theorem 3.23
.

The following bounds of
(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)
hold for
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
.

(a)

For
N\geq 0
,

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)\geq\frac{1}{2^{N}}e^{-tN^{2}}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad t>0.

(b)

For
N\geq 1
and
\epsilon\in(0,1/3]
,

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\Big(1+\frac{21}{500}\Big)\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\Big[\frac{\pi}{4}(1+\epsilon)\Big]^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{27\pi^{3}\epsilon}{512(N-1/2)}.

(c)

For
N\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,N]
,

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\Big(1+\frac{1}{1000}\Big)e^{\delta}\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)^{N}t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{\delta}{4N^{2}}.

(d)

For
N=0
and
N=1
,

\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\Big[1+2e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}\Big]W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad-D\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<\frac{\pi}{4}t^{-1}W_{t}(\varphi),\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}.

Items (b)–(d) of Theorem
3.23
, with the choices of specific
\epsilon=256/(27\pi^{3})\approx 0.305792

and
\delta=1
in (b) and (c), respectively, imply the following.

Corollary 3.24
.

Let
N\geq 0
. Then

(-D)^{N}\vartheta_{t}(\varphi)<t^{-N}W_{t}(\varphi)\times\begin{cases}\mathfrak{w}_{0}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}^{N},&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;0<t\leq\frac{1}{2N+1}\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;N\geq 1\\
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\,({\pi}/4)^{N},&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;0<t\leq\frac{1}{(2N+1)^{2}}\end{cases},\qquad\varphi\in[0,\pi/2],

where
\mathfrak{w}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{w}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}
are numerical constants given by
18
18

18

Note that
\mathfrak{w}_{0}\approx 0.797983
,
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 2.721
,
\mathfrak{w}_{1}\approx 1.025567

and
\pi/4\approx 0.785398
.

\mathfrak{w}_{0}:=\Big(1+\frac{21}{500}\Big)\frac{1}{1+256/(27\pi^{3})},\qquad\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}:=\Big(1+\frac{1}{1000}\Big)e,\qquad\mathfrak{w}_{1}:=\frac{\pi}{4}\Big(1+\frac{256}{27\pi^{3}}\Big).

Combining Theorem
3.23
(a) and Corollary
3.24
with (
3.4
) we conclude bounds for
G^{\lambda}_{t}(x,1)

when
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2
and
x\in[0,1]
.

Corollary 3.25
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2
. The following bounds of
G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\varphi,1)
hold for
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
.

(a)

G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\varphi,1)\geq\frac{1}{4^{\lambda+1/2}\Gamma(\lambda+1)}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad t>0.

(b)

\displaystyle G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\varphi,1)

\displaystyle<\frac{\mathfrak{w}_{0}(\mathfrak{w}_{1}/2)^{\lambda+1/2}}{\Gamma(\lambda+1)}e^{t(\lambda+1/2)^{2}}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{w}_{0}(\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}/2)^{\lambda+1/2}}{\Gamma(\lambda+1)}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda+2},\quad\lambda\neq-1/2.

(c)

\displaystyle G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\varphi,1)

\displaystyle<\frac{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/8)^{\lambda+1/2}}{\Gamma(\lambda+1)}e^{t(\lambda+1/2)^{2}}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/8)^{\lambda+1/2}}{\Gamma(\lambda+1)}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{1}{(2\lambda+2)^{2}}.

The discrepancy ratios in Corollary
3.25
between the upper and the lower bounds are equal

\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{\lambda+1/2}
in case
t\leq 1/(2\lambda+2)
and

\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{\lambda+1/2}
in case
t\leq 1/(2\lambda+2)^{2}
.
19
19

19

Note that
2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}\approx 3.381748
,
\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 3.493833

and
\pi/2\approx 1.570796
.

Finally, we state more precise upper bounds for the special cases
\lambda=\pm 1/2
, see Theorem
3.23
(d).
The lower bounds in Corollary
3.26
are just specifications to
\lambda=\pm 1/2
of the bound from
Corollary
3.25
(a).

Corollary 3.26
.

The following bounds of
G_{t}^{\pm 1/2}(\cos\varphi,1)
hold for
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{1}{t^{1/2}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)}

\displaystyle\leq G_{t}^{-1/2}(\cos\varphi,1)<\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big[1+2e^{-\pi^{2}/(2t)}\Big]\frac{1}{t^{1/2}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi}}\frac{1}{t^{3/2}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)}

\displaystyle\leq G_{t}^{1/2}(\cos\varphi,1)<\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{4}e^{t}\frac{1}{t^{3/2}}e^{-\varphi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}.

Moreover, the lower bounds of
G_{t}^{\pm 1/2}(\cos\varphi,1)
hold also for
t>\pi^{2}/2
.

4.
The unified proof

In what follows, in each of the Steps A–F we express the multiplicative constants in terms of the multiplicative
constants from the previous ‘father’ step (the father for Step A is Section
3
),
so that later all the dependence can be traced back to the beginning. Then also each step can be analyzed independently or, perhaps,
in the future independently refined. In this sense Steps A–F together with Section
3
are independent modules
constituting the unified proof.

4.1.
Step A

Estimates of
G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)
for
x\in[0,1]
,
t\leq T_{A}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2
.

This step is based on the results of Section
3
.

Lemma A
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2
and
T_{A}>0
. Then

G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{C^{A}_{\lambda,T_{A}}\atop c^{A}_{\lambda,T_{A}}\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
and
0<t\leq T_{A}
, with the constant

c^{A}_{\lambda,T_{A}}\equiv c_{\lambda,\infty}^{A}:=\frac{1}{4^{\lambda+1/2}\Gamma(\lambda+1)}

and some upper multiplicative constant
C_{\lambda,T_{A}}^{A}
which can be specified according to choices of
\lambda
and
T_{A}

as follows:

C^{A}_{\lambda,T_{A}}:=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\lambda+1)}\times\begin{cases}\mathfrak{w}_{0}(\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}/2)^{\lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;\lambda\neq\pm 1/2\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;T_{A}=1/(2\lambda+2)\\
\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/8)^{\lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;\lambda\neq\pm 1/2\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;T_{A}=1/(2\lambda+2)^{2}\\
1+2\exp(-\pi^{2}/(2T_{A}))&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;\lambda=-1/2\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;T_{A}\leq\pi^{2}/2\\
\pi\exp(T_{A})/8&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;\lambda=1/2\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;T_{A}\leq\pi^{2}/2\end{cases}.

The first line in the above formula gives correct
C^{A}_{\lambda,T_{A}}
also in case
\lambda=1/2
, whereas
the second line gives correct
C^{A}_{\lambda,T_{A}}
in both cases
\lambda=\pm 1/2
,
but the last two lines provide stronger results for these
\lambda
.

Proof.

The lower bound is just Corollary
3.25
(a). The upper bounds with specified multiplicative constants follow
from items (b) and (c) in Corollary
3.25
and Corollary
3.26
.
The upper bound with unspecified multiplicative constant and no constraints on
T_{A}
holds
20
20

20

A posteriori
the upper bound with no constraints on
T_{A}
also follows from the unified proof with constrained
T_{A}
, see
the discussion in the beginning of Section
5
.

because of Theorem
1
.
∎

Remark 4.1
.

To proceed with the remaining steps of the unified proof, in Step A it would be sufficient to estimate the even part
of the kernel,
[G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)]_{\textrm{even}}=[G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)+G_{t}^{\lambda}(-x,1)]/2
, which could potentially have
better upper bounds, but worse lower bounds.
Note that
G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)/2\leq[G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)]_{\textrm{even}}\leq G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)
for
x\in[0,1]
,
the second inequality since
x\mapsto G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)
is increasing on
[-1,1]
, see Lemma
2.1
.

4.2.
Step B

Estimates of
G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,1)

for
x\in[0,1]
,
t\leq T_{B}
and
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
such that
\alpha+\beta\in\mathbb{N}
.

This step is based on Step A. The relevant technical tools are Lemma
2.3
(i) and Lemma
2.14
.

Lemma B
.

Let
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
be such that
\alpha+\beta\in\mathbb{N}
and let
T_{B}>0
. Then

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{C^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}\atop c^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\alpha+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
and
0<t\leq T_{B}
, with the constants

\displaystyle c^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}

\displaystyle:={c}^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B}/4}\times\frac{4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}b_{\beta}}{\big[\mathbb{D}_{\beta}T_{B}\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big]^{\beta+1/2}}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},

\displaystyle C^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}

\displaystyle:={C}^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B}/4}\times\frac{4^{\alpha+\beta+2}B_{\beta}}{(\mathfrak{b}\pi^{2}/2)^{\beta+1/2}}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

When referring to Lemma
B
in the ultraspherical case
\lambda=\alpha=\beta
, notation of the resulting constants

c^{B}_{\lambda,\lambda,T_{B}}
,
C^{B}_{\lambda,\lambda,T_{B}}
will be abbreviated to
c^{B}_{\lambda,T_{B}}
,
C^{B}_{\lambda,T_{B}}
, respectively.

Proof of Lemma
B
.

Fix
T_{B}>0
. Along the proof we consider
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
and
0<t\leq T_{B}
.
By Lemma
2.3
(i)

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)

\displaystyle=\int G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(v\cos\frac{\theta}{2},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v)

\displaystyle\simeq\left\{2\atop 1\right\}\int_{[0,1]}{G}_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(v\cos\frac{\theta}{2},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v),

the last relation by monotonicity of the kernel in the first variable (see Lemma
2.1
) and symmetry of the measure.

Observing now that
v\cos\frac{\theta}{2}=\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,0}(1,v)}
with
F
defined in (
2.4
),
and then using Lemma
A
, we get

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{2{C}^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B}/4}\atop{c}^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B}/4}\right\}\,\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\int_{[0,1]}\exp\bigg(-\frac{F_{\theta,0}(1,v)}{t}\bigg)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v).

To estimate the last integral we use Lemma
2.14
obtaining

\int_{[0,1]}\exp\bigg(-\frac{F_{\theta,0}(1,v)}{t}\bigg)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v)\simeq\left\{B_{\beta}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)\atop b_{\beta}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)\right\}t^{\beta+1/2}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg);

recall, see (
2.10
), that
\Psi_{\beta}^{\kappa}(t,\theta,\varphi)=[\mathbb{D}_{\beta}t\vee\kappa\theta\varphi]^{-\beta-1/2}
.
Since

\displaystyle\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle\leq\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi/2,\pi)=[\mathbb{D}_{\beta}t\vee\mathfrak{b}\pi^{2}/2]^{-\beta-1/2}\leq(\mathfrak{b}\pi^{2}/2)^{-\beta-1/2},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle\geq\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi,\pi)=[\mathbb{D}_{\beta}t\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}]^{-\beta-1/2}\geq[\mathbb{D}_{\beta}T_{B}\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}]^{-\beta-1/2},

we arrive at the bounds

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{2C^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B/4}}B_{\beta}(\mathfrak{b}\pi^{2}/2)^{-\beta-1/2}\atop c^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B}/4}b_{\beta}[\mathbb{D}_{\beta}T_{B}\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}]^{-\beta-1/2}\right\}\frac{4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}}{t^{\alpha+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg).

The lemma follows.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

The proof of Lemma
B
shows that the result could be strengthened by keeping, instead of estimating, the factors

\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi/2,\pi)
and
\psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi,\pi)
. Then the bounds would have the form

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{\widetilde{C}^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi/2,\pi)\atop\widetilde{c}^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi,\pi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\alpha+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg),

with the constants

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}

\displaystyle:={c}^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B}/4}\times{4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}b_{\beta}}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}^{B}_{\alpha,\beta,T_{B}}

\displaystyle:={C}^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T_{B}/4}\times{4^{\alpha+\beta+2}B_{\beta}}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

4.3.
Step C

Estimates of
G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)
for
x\in[0,1]
,
t\leq T_{C}
and
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
.

This step is based on Step B and Lemma
2.4
.
Note that
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}+1/2
is already covered by Lemma
A
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
is covered by
Lemma
B
.

Denote

\displaystyle\omega_{\lambda}(t)

\displaystyle:=2^{\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2-\lambda}e^{(\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2)(\lambda+\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2+1)t},

\displaystyle\Omega_{\lambda}(t)

\displaystyle:=2^{\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2-\lambda-1/2}e^{(\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2+1/2)(\lambda+\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2+1/2)t}.

Observe that for
\lambda>0
the function
\omega_{\lambda}(t)
is non-increasing in
t
, while
\Omega_{\lambda}(t)
is increasing.
Moreover,

(4.1)

e^{-(\lambda+3/4)t}\leq\omega_{\lambda}(t),\qquad\Omega_{\lambda}(t)\leq e^{(\lambda+1/2)t},\qquad\lambda,t>0,

Notice that the relation
\omega_{\lambda}(t)\leq\Omega_{\lambda}(t)
is not true in general.

Lemma C
.

Let
\lambda>0
and let
T_{C}>0
. Then

G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{C^{C}_{\lambda,T_{C}}\atop c^{C}_{\lambda,T_{C}}\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
and
0<t\leq T_{C}
, with the constants

c^{C}_{\lambda,T_{C}}:=c^{B}_{\lambda,\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda,T_{C}}\times\omega_{\lambda}(T_{C}),\qquad C^{C}_{\lambda,T_{C}}:=C^{B}_{\lambda,\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda-1,T_{C}}\times\Omega_{\lambda}(T_{C}).

Proof.

To begin with, we observe that Lemma
2.4
specified to
\epsilon=0
implies the bound

(4.2)

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta+\delta}(\cos\theta,1)\leq 2^{-\delta/2}e^{\frac{\delta}{2}(\alpha+\beta+1+\frac{\delta}{2})t}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1),\qquad\theta\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t>0,

where
\alpha,\beta>-1
,
\delta\geq 0
and
\beta\geq-\delta/2
(the last assumption only if
\delta>0
).

Throughout the proof
\lambda>0
and we consider
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that

N<2\lambda\leq N+1,

that is
N=\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-1
.

Take
\alpha=\beta=\lambda
and
\delta=N+1-2\lambda
.
Clearly,
\delta\geq 0
and
\beta>-\delta/2
.
Since with the parameters chosen the assumptions for (
4.2
) are satisfied, we get the bound
(notice that
\delta/2=\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2-\lambda
)

G_{t}^{\lambda,N+1-\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{\lambda}(t)}G_{t}^{\lambda,\lambda}(\cos\theta,1),\qquad\theta\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t>0.

Observing that
\lambda+(N+1-\lambda)=N+1\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}

and using the lower bound from Lemma
B
leads to the estimate

G_{t}^{\lambda,\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\geq\omega_{\lambda}(T_{C})c^{B}_{\lambda,\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda,T_{C}}\,\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)

that holds for
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
and
t\in(0,T_{C}]
.
This is the lower bound of the lemma.

Next, take
\alpha=\lambda
and
\beta=\lambda-\delta
, where
\delta=2\lambda-N
.
Obviously,
\delta>0
. Further,
\beta\geq-1/2
(this is equivalent to
\lambda\leq N+1/2
, which is true since
2\lambda\leq N+1
;
notice that
\beta=-1/2
only when
\lambda=1/2
, the case effectively covered by Lemma
A
)
and
\beta\geq-\delta/2
as can be easily checked.
Thus we can use (
4.2
) to write (notice that
\delta/2=\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil/2+1/2
)

G_{t}^{\lambda,\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\leq\Omega_{\lambda}(t)G_{t}^{\lambda,N-\lambda}(\cos\theta,1),\qquad\theta\in[0,\pi/2],\quad t>0.

Observing that
\lambda+(N-\lambda)=N\in\mathbb{N}
and making use of the upper bound in Lemma
B
leads to

G_{t}^{\lambda,\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\leq\Omega_{\lambda}(T_{C})C^{B}_{\lambda,\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda-1,T_{C}}\,\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)

for
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
and
t\in(0,T_{C}]
.
This is the upper bound of the lemma.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

Lemma
C
could be stated in a simpler, though weaker, form with
\omega_{\lambda}(T_{C})
and
\Omega_{\lambda}(T_{C})

replaced by the bounds from (
4.1
). On the other hand, the result could be strengthened by keeping factors
in
t
. Further strengthening is possible by combining the proof of Lemma
C
with Remark
4.2
, which would result
in the bounds

G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{\widetilde{C}^{B}_{\lambda,\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda-1,T_{C}}\Psi^{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda-1}(t,\pi/2,\pi)\,\Omega_{\lambda}(t)\atop\widetilde{c}^{B}_{\lambda,\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda,T_{C}}\Psi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda}(t,\pi,\pi)\,\omega_{\lambda}(t)\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}\exp\bigg({-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
and
0<t\leq T_{C}
; here
\lambda>0
.

4.4.
Step D

Estimates of
G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)
for
x\in[0,1]
,
t\leq T_{D}
and
\lambda\in(-1,0)
.

This step is based on Step C and Lemma
2.1
, the heat equation (
2.1
), and Lemma
2.9
.
See the proof of
[
29
, Lem. 5.1]
.
Note that in Lemma
D
below we could get better constants in the case
\lambda=-1/2
by referring in the proof
to Lemma
A
instead of Lemma
C
, but we leave this improvement aside since Lemma
D
will
not be used for this particular value of
\lambda
.

Lemma D
.

Let
-1<\lambda<0
and let
T_{D}>0
. Then

{G}_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{C^{D}_{\lambda,T_{D}}\atop c^{D}_{\lambda,T_{D}}\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
and
0<t\leq T_{D}
, with the constants

\displaystyle c^{D}_{\lambda,T_{D}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{8(\lambda+1)}{\pi}e^{-(2\lambda+2)T_{D}}\bigg[c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}\wedge\bigg(c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}\times m_{\lambda+2}\times\frac{8(\lambda+2)}{\pi}e^{-(2\lambda+4)T_{D}}\bigg)\bigg],

\displaystyle C^{D}_{\lambda,T_{D}}

\displaystyle:=4(\lambda+1)\bigg[C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}\vee\bigg(C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}\times M_{\lambda+2}\times\frac{16(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\times\Big(\frac{16T_{D}}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}\bigg)\bigg].

Proof.

We first estimate
G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)
. Recalling that
G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)
satisfies the heat equation (
2.1
) based on the
Jacobi-ultraspherical operator
J^{\lambda}
and using Lemma
2.1
twice we can write

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)=-J^{\lambda}_{x}G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)\big|_{x=0}=\frac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}x^{2}}G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)\big|_{x=0}=4(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)e^{-t(4\lambda+6)}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(0,1).

Estimating the last expression with the aid of Lemma
C
we get

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)\simeq\left\{C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}\atop c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{D}}\right\}\,4(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)t^{-\lambda-3}e^{-{\pi^{2}}/{(16t)}},\qquad t\leq T_{D}.

Then, since
G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)\to 0
as
t\to 0^{+}
(this follows from the above by an argument given in the proof of

[
29
, Lem. 5.1]
; it can also be seen as a consequence of
[
29
, Lem. 5.1]
), we have

G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}G_{\tau}^{\lambda}(0,1)\,{\rm d}\tau\simeq\left\{C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}\atop c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{D}}\right\}\,4(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{-\lambda-3}e^{-{\pi^{2}}/{(16\tau)}}\,{\rm d}\tau.

Here in the last integral we change the variable and then apply Lemma
2.9
getting

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{-\lambda-3}e^{-{\pi^{2}}/{(16\tau)}}\,{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}\Big)^{\lambda+2}\int_{\frac{\pi^{2}}{16t}}^{\infty}u^{(\lambda+2)-1}e^{-u}\,{\rm d}u

(4.3)

\displaystyle\simeq\left\{M_{\lambda+2}\atop m_{\lambda+2}\right\}\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}\Big(\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}t+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\pi^{2}}/{(16t)}}

for
t\leq T_{D}
. It follows that

(4.4)

\begin{split}G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)&\simeq\left\{C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}M_{\lambda+2}(16T_{D}/\pi^{2}+1)^{\lambda+1}\atop c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}m_{\lambda+2}e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{D}}\right\}\,\\
&\qquad\times\frac{64(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,e^{-{[(\pi/2)^{2}-\theta^{2}]}/{(4t)}}\,t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}},\qquad t\leq T_{D}\end{split}

(here we artificially insert factors containing
\theta
for the sake of clarity when utilizing these bounds in the sequel).

Next, we estimate
G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)
for
x\in[0,1]
and
t\leq T_{D}
.
Using Lemma
2.1
we write

(4.5)

G_{t}^{\lambda}(x,1)=G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)+\int_{0}^{x}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}y}G_{t}^{\lambda}(y,1)\,{\rm d}y=G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)+2(\lambda+1)e^{-t(2\lambda+2)}\int_{0}^{x}G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(y,1)\,{\rm d}y.

To proceed, we focus on the last integral. For
x=\cos\theta
,
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
, we have

\int_{0}^{x}G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(y,1)\,{\rm d}y=\int_{\theta}^{\pi/2}G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(\cos\varphi,1)\sin\varphi\,{\rm d}\varphi\simeq\left\{1\atop 2/\pi\right\}\,\int_{\theta}^{\pi/2}G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(\cos\varphi,1)\,\varphi\,{\rm d}\varphi.

Now Lemma
C
comes into play giving

\int_{0}^{x}G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(y,1)\,{\rm d}y\simeq\left\{C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}\atop c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}{2}/{\pi}\right\}t^{-\lambda-2}\int_{\theta}^{\pi/2}e^{-{\varphi^{2}}/{(4t)}}\varphi\,{\rm d}\varphi,\qquad t\leq T_{D}.

Evaluating the last integral, we obtain

(4.6)

\int_{0}^{x}G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(y,1)\,{\rm d}y\simeq\left\{C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}\atop c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}{2}/{\pi}\right\}\,2\Big(1-e^{-{[(\pi/2)^{2}-\theta^{2}]}/{{4t}}}\Big)\,t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}},\qquad t\leq T_{D}.

Combining (
4.5
) with (
4.4
) and (
4.6
) produces

\displaystyle\bigg[\Big(c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}m_{\lambda+2}\frac{64(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{D}}\Big)\wedge\bigg(\frac{8(\lambda+1)}{\pi}c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}e^{-(2\lambda+2)T_{D}}\bigg)\bigg]\,t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}}

\displaystyle\leq G_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\theta,1)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\bigg(C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T_{D}}M_{\lambda+2}\frac{64(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\Big[\frac{16T_{D}}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big]^{\lambda+1}\bigg)\vee\Big(4(\lambda+1)C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T_{D}}\Big)\bigg]\,t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}}

for
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
and
t\leq T_{D}
.
The lemma follows.
∎

4.5.
Step E

Estimates of
H_{t}^{\lambda}(x)
for
x\in[0,1]
,
t\leq T_{E}
and
\lambda\in(-3/2,-1]
.

This step is based essentially on Step C (with Step B being involved only to get better multiplicative constants in a certain case)
and Lemma
2.1
, Lemma
2.2
, the heat equation (
2.2
), and Lemma
2.9
.
Step D is also of some relevance. See the proof of
[
29
, Lem. 5.3]
.

In the statement below, and also in its proof, we use the notation
c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda,T}
,

C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda,T}
, where
\blacklozenge=B
if
\lambda=1
and
\blacklozenge=C
if
\lambda\neq 1
.

Lemma E
.

Let
-3/2<\lambda\leq-1
and let
T_{E}>0
. Then

H_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\theta)\simeq\left\{C^{E}_{\lambda,T_{E}}\atop c^{E}_{\lambda,T_{E}}\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\lambda+1}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
and
0<t\leq T_{E}
, with the constants

\displaystyle c^{E}_{\lambda,T_{E}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{32(1-1/e)}{\pi^{2}}(\lambda+2)e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{E}}\,c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\times\bigg[2m_{\lambda+2}\Big(\frac{16T_{E}}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}\wedge 1\bigg],

\displaystyle C^{E}_{\lambda,T_{E}}

\displaystyle:=16(\lambda+2)\,C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\times\bigg[\frac{4}{\pi^{2}}M_{\lambda+2}\vee 1\bigg].

Proof.

We first estimate
H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)
similarly as
G_{t}^{\lambda}(0,1)
in the proof of Lemma
D
.
Using the fact that
H_{t}^{\lambda}
satisfies the heat equation based on the Jacobi-ultraspherical operator

J^{\lambda}
, see (
2.2
), and then Lemma
2.2
, we write

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)=-J^{\lambda}H_{t}^{\lambda}(x)\big|_{x=0}=\frac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}x^{2}}H_{t}^{\lambda}(x)\big|_{x=0}=4(\lambda+2)e^{-t(4\lambda+6)}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(0,1).

Estimating the last expression with the aid of Lemmas
B
and
C
we get

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)\simeq\left\{C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\atop c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\right\}\,4(\lambda+2)e^{-t(4\lambda+6)}t^{-\lambda-3}e^{-{\pi^{2}}/{(16t)}},\qquad t\leq T_{E}.

Taking into account that
H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)\to 0
as
t\to 0^{+}
(this follows by Lemma
2.3
(v),
Lemma
2.1
, Lemma
D
, and an argument given in the proof of
[
29
, Lem. 5.3]
; it is also a consequence
of
[
29
, Lem. 5.3]
) we infer that

H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}H_{\tau}^{\lambda}(0)\,{\rm d}\tau\simeq\left\{C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\atop c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{E}}\right\}4(\lambda+2)\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{-\lambda-3}e^{-{\pi^{2}}/{(16\tau)}}\,{\rm d}\tau.

Then, estimating as in (
4.3
) with the aid of Lemma
2.9
, we get

(4.7)

\begin{split}H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)&\simeq\left\{C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}M_{\lambda+2}\atop c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}m_{\lambda+2}(16T_{E}/\pi^{2}+1)^{\lambda+1}e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{E}}\right\}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{64(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}e^{-{[(\pi/2)^{2}-\theta^{2}]}/{(4t)}}\,t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}},\qquad t\leq T_{E}\end{split}

(here we artificially write factors containing
\theta
for the sake of clarity when utilizing these bounds in the sequel).

Now we estimate
H_{t}^{\lambda}(x)
for
x\in[0,1]
and
t\leq T_{E}
.
Using Lemma
2.2
and then Lemma
2.1
we get

\displaystyle H_{t}^{\lambda}(x)

\displaystyle=H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)+\int_{0}^{x}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}y}H_{t}^{\lambda}(y)\,{\rm d}y=H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)+2e^{-t(2\lambda+2)}\int_{0}^{x}\Big[G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(y,1)\Big]_{\textrm{odd}}\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)+e^{-t(2\lambda+2)}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{-y}^{y}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}z}G_{t}^{\lambda+1}(z,1)\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)+2(\lambda+2)e^{-t(4\lambda+6)}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{-y}^{y}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(z,1)\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}y

(4.8)

\displaystyle\simeq H_{t}^{\lambda}(0)+\left\{2\atop 1\right\}2(\lambda+2)e^{-t(4\lambda+6)}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{y}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(z,1)\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}y,

the last relation by monotonicity of
z\mapsto G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(z,1)
, see Lemma
2.1
.

To proceed, we focus on the last double integral. Letting
x=\cos\theta
,
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{y}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(z,1)\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{\theta}^{\pi/2}\int_{\varphi}^{\pi/2}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(\cos\kappa,1)\sin\kappa\,{\rm d}\kappa\,\sin\varphi\,{\rm d}\varphi

\displaystyle\simeq\left\{1\atop(2/\pi)^{2}\right\}\int_{\theta}^{\pi/2}\int_{\varphi}^{\pi/2}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(\cos\kappa,1)\,\kappa\,{\rm d}\kappa\,\varphi\,{\rm d}\varphi.

Then, by Lemmas
B
and
C
,

\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{y}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(z,1)\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}y\simeq\left\{C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\atop c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}(2/\pi)^{2}\right\}t^{-\lambda-3}\int_{\theta}^{\pi/2}\int_{\varphi}^{\pi/2}e^{-{\kappa^{2}}/{(4t)}}\,\kappa\,{\rm d}\kappa\,\varphi\,{\rm d}\varphi.

Evaluating the last double integral gives

(4.9)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{y}G_{t}^{\lambda+2}(z,1)\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}y\simeq\left\{C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\atop c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}(2/\pi)^{2}\right\}\\
&\qquad\times 4\bigg[1-\bigg(1+\frac{(\pi/2)^{2}-\theta^{2}}{4t}\bigg)e^{-{[(\pi/2)^{2}-\theta^{2}]}/{(4t)}}\bigg]t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}},\qquad t\leq T_{E}.\end{split}

It remains to combine (
4.8
) with (
4.7
) and (
4.9
), observing that range of the function

[0,\infty)\ni\eta\mapsto e^{-\eta}+1-(1+\eta)e^{-\eta}=1-\eta e^{-\eta}
is
[1-1/e,1]

(think
\eta=[{(\pi/2)^{2}-\theta^{2}}]/{(4t)}
). We get

\displaystyle\frac{32(1-1/e)}{\pi^{2}}(\lambda+2)e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{E}}\,c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\times\bigg[2m_{\lambda+2}\Big(\frac{16T_{E}}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}\wedge 1\bigg]t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}}

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}^{\lambda}(\cos\theta)\leq 16(\lambda+2)\,C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}\times\bigg[\frac{4}{\pi^{2}}M_{\lambda+2}\vee 1\bigg]t^{-\lambda-1}e^{-{\theta^{2}}/{(4t)}}

for
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
and
t\leq T_{E}
. The conclusion follows.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

Let
-3/2<\lambda\leq-1
and let
T_{E}>0
.
Then

\displaystyle 2m_{\lambda+2}\Big(\frac{16T_{E}}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}\wedge 1

\displaystyle=2m_{\lambda+2}\Big(\frac{16T_{E}}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1},

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\pi^{2}}M_{\lambda+2}\vee 1

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\pi^{2}}M_{\lambda+2},

and consequently

\displaystyle c^{E}_{\lambda,T_{E}}

\displaystyle=\frac{64(1-1/e)}{\pi^{2}}(\lambda+2)e^{-(4\lambda+6)T_{E}}\,c^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}m_{\lambda+2}\Big(\frac{16T_{E}}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1},

\displaystyle C^{E}_{\lambda,T_{E}}

\displaystyle=\frac{64(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,C^{\blacklozenge}_{\lambda+2,T_{E}}M_{\lambda+2}.

To justify this, observe that
2m_{\lambda+2}\leq 2m_{1/2}<1
, since
m_{\lambda}
is decreasing.
On the other hand, using the bound
\pi(1-1/e)\leq 2
we get
{4}{\pi^{-2}}M_{\lambda+2}\geq 1

in the range of
\lambda
in question.

We do not include the simplified constants in Lemma
E
to keep its more abstract form that is convenient for potential future
improvements. More precisely, Remark
4.4
is based on particular, perhaps non-optimal,
constants delivered by Lemma
2.9
and might not hold once they are refined.

4.6.
Step F

Estimates of
G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)
for
x,y\in[-1,1]
,
t\leq T
and
\alpha,\beta>-1
.

This final step is based essentially on Steps C–E, see
[
29
, Sec. 6]
, however Steps A and B will also be involved to get better
multiplicative constants in certain cases. The technical tools that are relevant for Step F are the pivotal Lemma
2.3

along with Lemmas
2.1
,
2.2
,
2.7
and
2.13
.

In the statement below, and also in its proof, we use the notation
c^{\bigstar}_{\lambda,T}
,
C^{\bigstar}_{\lambda,T}
, where

\bigstar=A
if
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2
,

\bigstar=B
if
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
,

\bigstar=C
if
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
and
\lambda\notin\mathbb{N}/2
,

\bigstar=D
if
-1/2\neq\lambda\in(-1,0)
, and

\bigstar=E
if
\lambda\in(-3/2,-1]
.
We will also use two auxiliary functions,

q_{\lambda}(t):=2(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)e^{-t(\lambda+3/2)},\qquad\widetilde{q}_{\lambda}(t):=2(\lambda+2)e^{-t(\lambda+3/2)},

and the notation

\Lambda=\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}:=\alpha+\beta+1/2.

Lemma F
.

Let
\alpha,\beta>-1
and let
T>0
. Then

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)\simeq\left\{C_{\alpha,\beta,T}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\atop c_{\alpha,\beta,T}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\exp\bigg({-\frac{(\theta-\varphi)^{2}}{4t}}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta,\varphi\leq\pi
and
0<t\leq T
, with the constants given as follows.

\blacktriangleright

In case
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
,

c_{\alpha,\beta,T}:=c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\times{4^{\Lambda+1}b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},\qquad C_{\alpha,\beta,T}:=C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\times{4^{\Lambda+2}B_{\alpha}B_{\beta}}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

\blacktriangleright

In case
-1<\beta<-1/2\leq\alpha
,

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+1/2}b_{\alpha}\bigg[c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}q_{\Lambda}(T)\Big(\frac{4\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\pi^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}}\Big)^{2}\frac{4}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}l_{\beta}b_{\beta+2}\wedge c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\bigg]\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+3/2}B_{\alpha}\bigg[C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}q_{\Lambda}(0)\frac{4}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}}L_{\beta}B_{\beta+2}+C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\bigg]\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

\blacktriangleright

In case
-1<\alpha<-1/2\leq\beta
,

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+1/2}b_{\beta}\bigg[c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}q_{\Lambda}(T)\Big(\frac{4\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}}{\pi^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha+2}}\Big)^{2}\frac{4}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}l_{\alpha}b_{\alpha+2}\wedge c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\bigg]\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+3/2}B_{\beta}\bigg[C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}q_{\Lambda}(0)\frac{4}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}}L_{\alpha}B_{\alpha+2}+C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\bigg]\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

\blacktriangleright

In case
-1<\alpha,\beta<-1/2
and
\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}>-1
,

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+1}\bigg[c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+4,T/4}q_{\Lambda+2}(T)q_{\Lambda}(T)\Big(\frac{2}{\pi}\Big)^{8}l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}b_{\alpha+2}b_{\beta+2}\Big(\frac{2\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha+2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}\mathfrak{B}^{2}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt\wedge c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}q_{\Lambda}(T)\Big(\frac{2}{\pi}\Big)^{4}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}\bigg\{\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}}{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha+2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}l_{\alpha}b_{\alpha+2}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt\wedge\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}l_{\beta}b_{\beta+2}\bigg\}\wedge c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\bigg]\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+2}\bigg[C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+4,T/4}q_{\Lambda+2}(0)q_{\Lambda}(0)\frac{4}{\mathfrak{b}^{4}}L_{\alpha}L_{\beta}B_{\alpha+2}B_{\beta+2}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt+C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}q_{\Lambda}(0)\frac{1}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}}\Big(L_{\alpha}B_{\alpha+2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}+L_{\beta}B_{\beta+2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt+C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\bigg]\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

\blacktriangleright

In case
-1<\alpha,\beta<-1/2
and
\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}\leq-1
,

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+1}\bigg[c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+4,T/4}q_{\Lambda+2}(T)\widetilde{q}_{\Lambda}(T)\Big(\frac{2}{\pi}\Big)^{8}l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}b_{\alpha+2}b_{\beta+2}\Big(\frac{2\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha+2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}\mathfrak{B}^{2}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt\wedge c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}\widetilde{q}_{\Lambda}(T)\Big(\frac{2}{\pi}\Big)^{4}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}\bigg\{\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}}{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha+2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}l_{\alpha}b_{\alpha+2}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt\wedge\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}l_{\beta}b_{\beta+2}\bigg\}\wedge c^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\bigg]\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma(\Lambda+2)}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}\Gamma(\Lambda+3/2)},

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=4^{\Lambda+2}\bigg[C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+4,T/4}q_{\Lambda+2}(0)\widetilde{q}_{\Lambda}(0)\frac{4}{\mathfrak{b}^{4}}L_{\alpha}L_{\beta}B_{\alpha+2}B_{\beta+2}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt+C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda+2,T/4}\widetilde{q}_{\Lambda}(0)\frac{1}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}}\Big(L_{\alpha}B_{\alpha+2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}+L_{\beta}B_{\beta+2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pt+C^{\bigstar}_{\Lambda,T/4}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\bigg]\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma(\Lambda+2)}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}\Gamma(\Lambda+3/2)}.

Proof of Lemma
F
, the case
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
.

By Lemma
2.3
(i)

{I}:=\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)=\iint G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v).

In view of monotonicity of
z\mapsto G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(z,1)
, see Lemma
2.1
, and symmetry of the measures
involved, this implies

{I}\simeq\left\{4\atop 1\right\}\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v).

Then under the last double integral
\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)}\in[0,\pi/2]
and we can use Lemmas

A
,
B
,
C
and
D
getting

{I}\simeq\left\{4C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\right\}\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}e^{-{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)}/{t}}\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v)

for
\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi]
and
0<t\leq T
.
Now an application of Lemma
2.13
shows that

{I}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}B_{\alpha}B_{\beta}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}

for
\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi]
and
0<t\leq T
. From this the estimates stated in the lemma follow for

\alpha
and
\beta
under consideration.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

Considering the case when
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
and
-1/2<\alpha+\beta\notin\mathbb{N}
, the bounds of Lemma
F

could be strengthened by employing in the proof the estimates from Remarks
4.2
and
4.3
instead of the bounds
from Lemmas
B
and
C
.
The details are straightforward and left to interested readers.

Proof of Lemma
F
, the cases when either
-1<\beta<-1/2\leq\alpha
or
-1<\alpha<-1/2\leq\beta
.

It is sufficient to prove the result only in the first case, for symmetry reasons. Then in the other case the result follows
by swapping
\alpha
with
\beta
and
\theta,\varphi
with
\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi
, respectively;
note that
G_{t}^{\beta,\alpha}(-x,-y)=G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)
.
Thus from now on we assume that
-1<\beta<-1/2\leq\alpha
.
All estimates we write in this proof are for
\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi]
and
0<t\leq T
.

By Lemma
2.3
(ii)

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)=2(\alpha+\beta+3/2)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+3/2)/2}I_{1}+I_{2},

where

(4.10)

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\iint G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,\Pi_{\beta}(v)\,{\rm d}v,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle:=\iint G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(v).

We will estimate
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
separately. Notice that both the quantities are positive, in case of
I_{1}
since

z\mapsto G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}(z,1)
is increasing (see Lemma
2.1
) and
\Pi_{\beta}
is odd and decreasing.

Concerning
I_{2}
, by monotonicity of
z\mapsto G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(z,1)
and symmetry of the measures involved,

I_{2}\simeq\left\{4\atop 1\right\}\,\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(v).

Estimating the integrand here by means of Lemmas
A
,
B
,
C
and
D
we get

I_{2}\simeq\left\{4C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\right\}\,\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}e^{-F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)/t}\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(v).

Now an application of Lemma
2.13
leads to

(4.11)

I_{2}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+2}B_{\alpha}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+1}b_{\alpha}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\beta+1}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}.

Passing to
I_{1}
, recall that
\Pi_{\beta}(v)
is an odd function which is negative for
v>0
. Thus

I_{1}=\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\int_{[-1,1]}\int_{[0,1]}\Big[G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\\
-G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,-v)},1\Big)\Big]\,|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u).

Here we can write the difference as the integral from
-v
to
v
of the derivative and then apply Lemma
2.1
. This gives

I_{1}=2(\alpha+\beta+5/2)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+5/2)/2}\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\\
\times\int_{[-1,1]}\int_{[0,1]}\int_{-v}^{v}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v^{\prime})},1\Big)\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\,|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u).

Denote the above triple integral by
\widetilde{I}_{1}
. Observe that the integrand there is increasing both in
u
and
v^{\prime}
,
see Lemma
2.1
. Hence, taking into account symmetries of the measures
{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}
and
{\rm d}v^{\prime}
, we may restrict
the integration in these two variables to
[0,1]
and
[0,v]
, respectively, getting

\widetilde{I}_{1}\simeq\left\{4\atop 1\right\}\,\int_{[0,1]}\int_{[0,1]}\int_{0}^{v}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v^{\prime})},1\Big)\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\,|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u).

Now we use Fubini’s theorem and integrate first in
v
, which produces a factor that can be estimated with the aid of
Lemma
2.7
,

\int_{v^{\prime}}^{1}|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v\simeq\left\{L_{\beta}\atop l_{\beta}\right\}\,\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta+2}(v^{\prime})}{{\rm d}v^{\prime}},\qquad v^{\prime}\in[0,1].

Consequently,

\widetilde{I}_{1}\simeq\left\{4L_{\beta}\atop l_{\beta}\right\}\,\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v^{\prime})},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta+2}(v^{\prime}).

From this, using Lemmas
A
,
B
and
C
, we obtain

\widetilde{I}_{1}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4L_{\beta}\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}l_{\beta}\right\}\,\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+\beta+7/2}\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}e^{-F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v^{\prime})/t}\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta+2}(v^{\prime}).

Finally, applying Lemma
2.13
we arrive at

(4.12)

\begin{split}\widetilde{I}_{1}&\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+9/2}L_{\beta}B_{\alpha}B_{\beta+2}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta+2}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+7/2}l_{\beta}b_{\alpha}b_{\beta+2}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta+2}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\right\}\,\\
&\qquad\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}.\end{split}

Next, we want to combine the bounds (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) for
I_{2}
and
\widetilde{I}_{1}
with the formula

(4.13)

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)=2q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(t)\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{I}_{1}+I_{2}.

To do that we need to bound suitably two auxiliary expressions.
For
\kappa>0
,
\lambda\in(-1,-1/2)
,
\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi]
and
t>0
consider

(4.14)

\displaystyle O_{1}^{\kappa,\lambda}=O_{1}^{\kappa,\lambda}(t,\theta,\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\Big[\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\times\frac{\Psi_{\lambda+2}^{\kappa}(t,\theta,\varphi)}{\Psi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}(t,\theta,\varphi)}

\displaystyle=\frac{[\sin({\theta}/2)\sin({\varphi}/2)]^{2}}{[\mathbb{D}_{\lambda+2}t\vee\kappa\theta\varphi]^{\lambda+5/2}[\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}t\vee\kappa\theta\varphi]^{-\lambda-1/2}},

(4.15)

\displaystyle O_{2}^{\kappa,\lambda}=O_{2}^{\kappa,\lambda}(t,\theta,\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\Psi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}(t,\theta,\varphi)t^{\lambda+1/2}}=\frac{1}{[\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}t\vee\kappa\theta\varphi]^{-\lambda-1/2}t^{\lambda+1/2}}.

Using the bounds
\theta/\pi\leq\sin(\theta/2)\leq\theta/2
for
\theta\in[0,\pi]

and the fact that
\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}
is increasing with respect to the parameter, we see that

\frac{1}{\pi^{4}}\frac{[\theta\varphi]^{2}}{[\mathbb{D}_{\lambda+2}t\vee\kappa\theta\varphi]^{2}}\leq O_{1}^{\kappa,\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{16}\frac{[\theta\varphi]^{2}}{[\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}t\vee\kappa\theta\varphi]^{2}}.

Notice that
O_{1}^{\kappa,\lambda}\leq 1/(16\kappa^{2})
and
O_{2}^{\kappa,\lambda}\leq\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}^{\lambda+1/2}
. Moreover,
if
\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}t\geq\kappa\theta\varphi
, then
O_{2}^{\kappa,\lambda}=\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}^{\lambda+1/2}
, and if

\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}t<\kappa\theta\varphi
, then
O_{1}^{\kappa,\lambda}\geq(\kappa\mathbb{D}_{\lambda+2}/\mathbb{D}_{\lambda})^{-2}/\pi^{4}
.
Therefore, for any
c_{1},c_{2}>0
,

(4.16)

\frac{c_{1}}{\pi^{4}}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}\kappa}\Big)^{2}\wedge c_{2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\leq c_{1}O_{1}^{\kappa,\beta}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)+c_{2}O_{2}^{\kappa,\beta}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\leq\frac{c_{1}}{16\kappa^{2}}+c_{2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}.

Combining (
4.13
) with (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) and using (
4.16
) with
\kappa=\mathfrak{b}
and

\kappa=\mathfrak{B}
we conclude that

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)\simeq\left\{\check{C}_{\alpha,\beta,T}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\atop\check{c}_{\alpha,\beta,T}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)},

where

\displaystyle\check{c}_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(T)}{\pi^{4}}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}\mathfrak{B}}\Big)^{2}c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+4}l_{\beta}b_{\alpha}b_{\beta+2}\wedge\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+1}b_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\check{C}_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(0)}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}}C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3}L_{\beta}B_{\alpha}B_{\beta+2}+\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+2}B_{\alpha}.

This implies the estimates stated in the lemma for
\alpha
and
\beta
under consideration.
∎

Proof of Lemma
F
, the case when
-1<\alpha,\beta<-1/2
.

All estimates we write in this part of the proof are for
\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi]
and
0<t\leq T
,
and the standing assumption is
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,-1/2)
.
We first estimate three quantities which are involved in the representations of
G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)
from
items (iv) and (v) of Lemma
2.3
. Let

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle:=\sin\theta\sin\varphi\iint G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}u\,\Pi_{\beta}(v)\,{\rm d}v,

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle:=-\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\iint G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,\Pi_{\alpha}(u)\,{\rm d}u\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(v),

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle:=-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\iint G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(u)\,\Pi_{\beta}(v)\,{\rm d}v.

Each of
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
is positive, as can be seen from the estimates that follow.

To treat
J_{1}
, recall that
\Pi_{\alpha}(u)
and
\Pi_{\beta}(v)
are odd functions of
u
and
v
, which are negative for

u>0
and
v>0
, respectively. Thus

J_{1}=\sin\theta\sin\varphi\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}\Delta(u,v)\,|\Pi_{\alpha}(u)|\,{\rm d}u\,|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v,

where
\Delta(u,v)
is the second difference given by

\displaystyle\Delta(u,v)

\displaystyle:=G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)-G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(-u,v)},1\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,-v)},1\Big)+G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(-u,-v)},1\Big).

Now the key observation is that

\Delta(u,v)=\iint_{|u^{\prime}|<u,\,|v^{\prime}|<v}\partial_{u^{\prime}}\partial_{v^{\prime}}\bigg[G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+5/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})},1\Big)\bigg]\,{\rm d}u^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}.

Applying Lemma
2.1
twice we see that the function under the double integral here is equal

\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha+\beta+5/2}(t)\sin\theta\sin\varphi\;G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+9/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})},1\Big).

We conclude that

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha+\beta+5/2}(t)\big(\sin\theta\sin\varphi)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}\iint_{|u^{\prime}|<u,\,|v^{\prime}|<v}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+9/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})},1\Big)\,{\rm d}u^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\,|\Pi_{\alpha}(u)|\,{\rm d}u\,|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v.

To proceed, denote the last quadruple integral by
\widetilde{J}_{1}
, so that

(4.17)

J_{1}=8q_{\alpha+\beta+5/2}(t)\Big[\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{1}.

The integrand in
\widetilde{J}_{1}
is increasing in both
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
(see Lemma
2.1
), hence we can restrict
the inner integration to
0<u^{\prime}<u
,
0<v^{\prime}<v
getting

\widetilde{J}_{1}\simeq\left\{4\atop 1\right\}\,\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{v}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+9/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})},1\Big)\,{\rm d}u^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\,|\Pi_{\alpha}(u)|\,{\rm d}u\,|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v.

Using now Fubini’s theorem to integrate first in
u
and
v
we obtain

\widetilde{J}_{1}\simeq\left\{4\atop 1\right\}\,\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{u^{\prime}}^{1}|\Pi_{\alpha}(u)|\,{\rm d}u\,\int_{v^{\prime}}^{1}|\Pi_{\beta}(v)|\,{\rm d}v\;G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+9/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})},1\Big)\,{\rm d}u^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}.

Then applying Lemma
2.7
twice we get

\widetilde{J}_{1}\simeq\left\{4L_{\alpha}L_{\beta}\atop l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}\right\}\,\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+9/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha+2}(u^{\prime})\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta+2}(v^{\prime}).

Further, making use of Lemmas
B
and
C
(notice that
5/2<\alpha+\beta+9/2<7/2
) leads to
