\widetilde{J}_{1}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+9/2,T/4}4L_{\alpha}L_{\beta}\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+9/2,T/4}l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}\right\}\,\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+\beta+11/2}\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}e^{-F_{\theta,\varphi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})/t}\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha+2}(u^{\prime})\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta+2}(v^{\prime}).

Eventually, applying Lemma
2.13
we arrive at

(4.18)

\begin{split}\widetilde{J}_{1}&\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+9/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+13/2}L_{\alpha}L_{\beta}B_{\alpha+2}B_{\beta+2}\Psi^{\mathfrak{b}}_{\alpha+2}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi^{\mathfrak{b}}_{\beta+2}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+9/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+11/2}l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}b_{\alpha+2}b_{\beta+2}\Psi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{\alpha+2}(t,\theta,\varphi)\Psi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{\beta+2}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\right\}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}.\end{split}

Next, we focus on
J_{3}
. This quantity is like
I_{1}
from (
4.10
). The only difference is that in
J_{3}
we have

{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(u)
instead of
{\rm d}\Pi_{\alpha}(u)
(with some
\alpha\geq-1/2
), but the analysis is exactly the same.
Thus

(4.19)

J_{3}=2(\alpha+\beta+5/2)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+5/2)/2}\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{3},

where

\widetilde{J}_{3}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4L_{\beta}\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}l_{\beta}\right\}\,\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+\beta+7/2}\iint_{[0,1]^{2}}e^{-F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v^{\prime})/t}\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta+2}(v^{\prime}).

Then, by Lemma
2.13
,

(4.20)

\widetilde{J}_{3}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+4}L_{\beta}B_{\beta+2}\Psi^{\mathfrak{b}}_{\beta+2}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3}l_{\beta}b_{\beta+2}\Psi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{\beta+2}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\alpha+1/2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}.

Finally, bounds for
J_{2}
follow from those for
J_{3}
by swapping
\alpha
with
\beta
and
\theta,\varphi
with
\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi
,
respectively. We get

(4.21)

J_{2}=2(\alpha+\beta+5/2)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+5/2)/2}\Big[\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{2},

where

(4.22)

\widetilde{J}_{2}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+4}L_{\alpha}B_{\alpha+2}\Psi^{\mathfrak{b}}_{\alpha+2}(t,\theta,\varphi)\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3}l_{\alpha}b_{\alpha+2}\Psi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{\alpha+2}(t,\theta,\varphi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\beta+1/2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}.

It is now convenient to consider two cases.

Case 1:
\alpha+\beta+1/2>-1
.
Here we also need to bound the quantity

J_{4}:=\iint G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(v)

appearing in Lemma
2.3
(iv). By monotonicity of
z\mapsto G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(z,1)
, see Lemma
2.1
,
we have

J_{4}\simeq\left\{1\atop 1/4\right\}\,G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(1,1)},1\Big).

Then Lemma
D
implies

(4.23)

J_{4}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\alpha+1/2}}\frac{1}{t^{\beta+1/2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}.

In view of Lemma
2.3
(iv), see also (
4.17
), (
4.19
) and (
4.21
),

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(t)J_{1}+2(\alpha+\beta+3/2)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+3/2)/2}(J_{2}+J_{3})+J_{4}

(4.24)

\displaystyle=4q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(t)q_{\alpha+\beta+5/2}(t)\Big[\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+2q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(t)\bigg(\Big[\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{2}+\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{3}\bigg)+J_{4}.

Taking into account the above and (
4.18
), (
4.20
), (
4.22
) and (
4.23
),
we see that in order to conclude the desired bounds for the Jacobi heat kernel it is necessary to estimate suitably an auxiliary
expression

\displaystyle\Upsilon

\displaystyle:=c_{1,1}O_{1}^{\kappa,\alpha}(t,\theta,\varphi)O_{1}^{\kappa,\beta}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)+c_{1,2}O_{1}^{\kappa,\alpha}(t,\theta,\varphi)O_{2}^{\kappa,\beta}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)

\displaystyle\quad+c_{2,1}O_{2}^{\kappa,\alpha}(t,\theta,\varphi)O_{1}^{\kappa,\beta}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)+c_{2,2}O_{2}^{\kappa,\alpha}(t,\theta,\varphi)O_{2}^{\kappa,\beta}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi),

where
c_{1,1},c_{1,2},c_{2,1},c_{2,2}
and
\kappa
are certain positive constants and
O_{1}^{\kappa,\alpha}
,
O_{2}^{\kappa,\alpha}

are defined in (
4.14
) and (
4.15
). The observations that led us to (
4.16
) now imply the bounds

\displaystyle\Upsilon

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1,1}}{\pi^{8}\kappa^{4}}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}}{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha+2}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}}\Big)^{2}\wedge\frac{c_{1,2}}{\pi^{4}\kappa^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}}{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha+2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}\wedge\frac{c_{2,1}}{\pi^{4}\kappa^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}{\mathbb{D}_{\beta+2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\wedge c_{2,2}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2},

\displaystyle\Upsilon

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{1,1}}{256\kappa^{4}}+\frac{c_{1,2}}{16\kappa^{2}}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}+\frac{c_{2,1}}{16\kappa^{2}}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}+{c_{2,2}}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}^{\alpha+1/2}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\beta+1/2}.

It is now enough to notice, see (
4.24
) and (
4.18
), (
4.20
), (
4.22
), (
4.23
),
that the lower bound of the lemma follows by taking

\displaystyle c_{1,1}

\displaystyle=4q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(T)q_{\alpha+\beta+5/2}(T)c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+9/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+11/2}l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}b_{\alpha+2}b_{\beta+2},

\displaystyle c_{1,2}

\displaystyle=2q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(T)c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3}l_{\alpha}b_{\alpha+2},

\displaystyle c_{2,1}

\displaystyle=2q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(T)c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3}l_{\beta}b_{\beta+2},

\displaystyle c_{2,2}

\displaystyle=c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+1/2},

while to get the upper bound one takes

\displaystyle c_{1,1}

\displaystyle=4q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(0)q_{\alpha+\beta+5/2}(0)C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+9/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+13/2}L_{\alpha}L_{\beta}B_{\alpha+2}B_{\beta+2},

\displaystyle c_{1,2}

\displaystyle=2q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(0)C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+4}L_{\alpha}B_{\alpha+2},

\displaystyle c_{2,1}

\displaystyle=2q_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(0)C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+5/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+4}L_{\beta}B_{\beta+2},

\displaystyle c_{2,2}

\displaystyle=C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}.

The conclusion follows.

Case 2:
\alpha+\beta+1/2\leq-1
.
Here we need to bound the additional quantity

J_{5}:=\iint H_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,\varphi}(u,v)}\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(u)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{-1/2}(v)

appearing in Lemma
2.3
(v). Since
z\mapsto H_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(z)
is even and increasing in
[0,1]

(see Lemma
2.2
), we have

J_{5}=\frac{1}{2}\bigg[H_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\frac{\theta-\varphi}{2}\Big)+H_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\frac{\theta+\varphi}{2}\Big)\bigg]\simeq\left\{2\atop 1\right\}\,\frac{1}{2}H_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\frac{\theta-\varphi}{2}\Big).

Then by Lemma
E

(4.25)

J_{5}\simeq\left\{C^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\atop c^{\bigstar}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}4^{\alpha+\beta+1}\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{\alpha+1/2}}\frac{1}{t^{\beta+1/2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)}.

In view of Lemma
2.3
(v),

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}\,\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)}{\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+5/2)}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{q}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(t)J_{1}+2e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+3/2)/2}(J_{2}+J_{3})+J_{5}

\displaystyle=4\widetilde{q}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(t)q_{\alpha+\beta+5/2}(t)\Big[\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+2\widetilde{q}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2}(t)\bigg(\Big[\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{2}\!+\Big[\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\frac{\varphi}{2}\Big]^{2}\widetilde{J}_{3}\bigg)+J_{5}.

Proceeding as in Case 1, but using now (
4.25
) instead of (
4.23
), we conclude for
\alpha
and
\beta
under consideration
the estimates for
G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)
stated in Lemma
F
.

The proof of Lemma
F
is complete.
∎

4.7.
Refinements in special cases

In this section we provide refinements of the bounds from Lemma
F
for
\varphi=0
in the cases when

\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
and
\alpha+\beta\in\mathbb{N}-1
or
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta=-1/2
.
These special cases are of interest because of their relevance for
estimates of the spherical heat kernel and, more generally, heat kernels on compact rank one symmetric spaces.

Lemma G
.

Let
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
be such that
\alpha+\beta\in\mathbb{N}-1
and let
T>0
. Then

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{C^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\beta,T}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)\atop c^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\beta,T}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{{t}^{\alpha+1}}\exp\bigg({-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi
and
0<t\leq T
, with the constants

\displaystyle c^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=c^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\times 4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}b_{\beta}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},

\displaystyle C^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle:=C^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\times 4^{\alpha+\beta+2}B_{\beta}\times\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
B
, by Lemma
2.3
(i) we have

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)

\displaystyle=\int G_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,0}(1,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v)

\displaystyle\simeq\left\{2\atop 1\right\}\int_{[0,1]}{G}_{t/4}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}\Big(\cos\sqrt{F_{\theta,0}(1,v)},1\Big)\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v).

Applying now Lemma
A
, which is legitimate due to the assumptions on
\alpha
and
\beta
, we see that

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{2C^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\atop c^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}\right\}\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\int_{[0,1]}e^{-F_{\theta,0}(1,v)/t}\,{\rm d}\Pi_{\beta}(v).

The last integral can be estimated by means of Lemma
2.14
. This gives

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+1/2}}G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{2C^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}B_{\beta}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)\atop c^{A}_{\alpha+\beta+1/2,T/4}b_{\beta}\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\theta,\pi)\right\}\frac{4^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}}{t^{\alpha+1}}\,e^{-\theta^{2}/(4t)}

for
\theta\in[0,\pi]
and
0<t\leq T
. The lemma follows.
∎

Lemma H
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
and let
T>0
. Then

G_{t}^{\alpha,-1/2}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{C^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,T}\atop c^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,T}\right\}\,\frac{1}{{t}^{\alpha+1}}\exp\bigg({-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4t}}\bigg)

for
0\leq\theta\leq\pi
and
0<t\leq T
, with the constants

\displaystyle c^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,T}

\displaystyle:=c^{A}_{2\alpha+1/2,T/16}\times 2\Big(\frac{32}{\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}T/4\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}}\Big)^{\alpha+1/2}b_{\alpha}\times\frac{h_{0}^{2\alpha+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha}},

\displaystyle C^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,T}

\displaystyle:=C^{A}_{2\alpha+1/2,T/16}\times 8\Big(\frac{64}{\mathfrak{b}\pi^{2}}\Big)^{\alpha+1/2}B_{\alpha}\times\frac{h_{0}^{2\alpha+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha}}.

Proof.

From
[
28
, Lem. 3.4]
we know that for
x,y\in[-1,1]
and
t>0

G_{t}^{\alpha,-1/2}\big(2x^{2}-1,2y^{2}-1\big)=2^{-\alpha-3/2}\big[G_{t/4}^{\alpha}(x,y)+G_{t/4}^{\alpha}(-x,y)\big].

This implies

G_{t}^{\alpha,-1/2}(\cos\theta,1)=\frac{1}{2^{\alpha+3/2}}\Big[G_{t/4}^{\alpha}\Big(\cos\frac{\theta}{2},1\Big)+G_{t/4}^{\alpha}\Big(-\cos\frac{\theta}{2},1\Big)\Big],\qquad\theta\in[0,\pi].

Using monotonicity of
z\mapsto G_{t/4}^{\alpha}(z,1)
, see Lemma
2.1
, we infer that

G_{t}^{\alpha,-1/2}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{2\atop 1\right\}\,\frac{1}{2^{\alpha+3/2}}G_{t/4}^{\alpha}\Big(\cos\frac{\theta}{2},1\Big).

Now Lemma
G
can be applied and we get

G_{t}^{\alpha,-1/2}(\cos\theta,1)\simeq\left\{2C^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\alpha,T/4}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t/4,\pi-\theta/2,\pi)\atop c^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\alpha,T/4}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t/4,\pi-\theta/2,\pi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{2^{\alpha+3/2}}\Big(\frac{4}{t}\Big)^{\alpha+1}e^{-\theta^{2}/(4t)}

for
\theta\in[0,\pi]
and
0<t\leq T
. Estimating

\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t/4,\pi-\theta/2,\pi)\leq\big(\mathfrak{b}\pi^{2}/2\big)^{-\alpha-1/2},\qquad\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t/4,\pi-\theta/2,\pi)\geq\big[\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}T/4\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big]^{-\alpha-1/2},

and inserting the formulas for
C^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\alpha,T/4}
and
c^{\textrm{ref}}_{\alpha,\alpha,T/4}
leads to the desired conclusion.
∎

5.
Large and medium time bounds

The unified proof from Section
4
provides sharp small time estimates for the Jacobi heat kernel,
with complete trace of multiplicative constants. In this section we complement that result by discussing medium and
large time bounds.

Let
\alpha,\beta>-1
and suppose that the sharp small time bounds from Lemma
F
hold for some fixed
t=t_{0}=T>0
.
Then it is straightforward to see that

(5.1)

G_{t_{0}}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\simeq\left\{\texttt{C}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\atop\texttt{c}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\right\},\qquad x,y\in[-1,1],

where the positive constants
\texttt{C}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}
and
\texttt{c}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}
express explicitly in terms of the
constants and other quantities appearing in the bounds of Lemma
F
.

On the other hand, if (
5.1
) holds for a fixed
t_{0}>0
(
t_{0}
presumably depending on
\alpha
and
\beta
), then also

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\simeq\left\{\texttt{C}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\atop\texttt{c}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\right\},\qquad x,y\in[-1,1],\quad\textrm{all}\;\;t\geq t_{0},

with the same constants. This is justified in the following well-known way.

Let
t>t_{0}
. By the semigroup property

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)=\int_{-1}^{1}G_{t-t_{0}}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,z)\,G_{t_{0}}^{\alpha,\beta}(z,y)\,{\rm d}\varrho_{\alpha,\beta}(z).

Therefore, in view of (
5.1
),

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\simeq\left\{\texttt{C}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\atop\texttt{c}_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\right\}\int_{-1}^{1}G_{t-t_{0}}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,z)\,{\rm d}\varrho_{\alpha,\beta}(z).

Now it remains to observe that the last integral is equal 1 since the Jacobi semigroup, being Markovian,
is in particular conservative.

Thus sharp medium and large time bounds for
G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)
are in the above sense covered by the sharp small time bounds.
The following statement is an instant consequence of the above considerations.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\alpha,\beta>-1
. Assume that the bounds of Lemma
F
hold with some
t_{0}=T>0
, that is

G_{t_{0}}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)\simeq\left\{C_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t_{0},\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t_{0},\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\atop c_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t_{0},\theta,\varphi)\Psi_{\beta}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t_{0},\pi-\theta,\pi-\varphi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{0}}}\exp\bigg({-\frac{(\theta-\varphi)^{2}}{4t_{0}}}\bigg)

for
\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi]
. Then, with the same constants
C_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}
and
c_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}
,

\displaystyle G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)

\displaystyle\simeq\left\{C_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\big(1\vee[1\vee 2/(\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}t_{0})]^{-\alpha-1/2}\big)\big(1\vee[1\vee 2/(\mathbb{D}_{\beta}t_{0})]^{-\beta-1/2}\big)\atop c_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}\big(1\wedge[1\vee\pi^{2}/(2\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}t_{0})]^{-\alpha-1/2}\big)\big(1\wedge[1\vee\pi^{2}/(2\mathbb{D}_{\beta}t_{0})]^{-\beta-1/2}\big)e^{-\pi^{2}/(4t_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{1}{t_{0}^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}\Gamma(\alpha+3/2)\Gamma(\beta+3/2)}

for
x,y\in[-1,1]
and all
t\geq t_{0}
.

The bounds of Proposition
5.1
can be simplified by distinguishing cases when
\alpha
and/or
\beta

are below
-1/2
or not. Then estimates with the aid of (
2.6
) lead to further simplifications.
The details are straightforward and left to interested readers.

Note that with
t_{0}\sim 1/(\alpha+\beta+3)
the ratio of the upper and lower bounds from Proposition
5.1
grows
no faster than exponentially in
\alpha
and
\beta
, assuming that the ratio
C_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}/c_{\alpha,\beta,t_{0}}
satisfies the same property.
As for the latter, this is indeed the case, at least when
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
; see Section
6.3
.

The above deliberations, however, may not provide satisfactorily precise estimates for large times.
Fortunately, for large
t
the bounds resulting from Proposition
5.1

can substantially be refined by exploiting the (oscillating) series representation of
G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)
.
This will be our objective for the remaining part of Section
5
.

In what follows we always assume
\alpha,\beta>-1
and
n\geq 0
; these assumptions can locally be strengthened.
Recall that

(5.2)

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-tn(n+\alpha+\beta+1)}\frac{P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(y)}{h_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}},

where

h_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}=\frac{2^{\alpha+\beta+1}\Gamma(n+\alpha+1)\Gamma(n+\beta+1)}{(2n+\alpha+\beta+1)\Gamma(n+\alpha+\beta+1)\Gamma(n+1)},

with appropriate convention when
n=0
and
\alpha+\beta+1=0
. In particular,

h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}=\frac{2^{\alpha+\beta+1}\Gamma(\alpha+1)\Gamma(\beta+1)}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)}.

By combining (
5.2
) with standard bounds for Jacobi polynomials it can be easily concluded that

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\to\frac{1}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},\qquad t\to\infty,

and the convergence is uniform in
x,y\in[-1,1]
.
Our aim is to make this convergence quantitative in terms of suitable estimates.

From (
5.2
) and the identity
n(n+\alpha+\beta+1)-(\alpha+\beta+2)=(n-1)(n+\alpha+\beta+2)
it follows that

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)=\frac{1}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}\Big[1+E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)\Big],

where

E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y):=e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+2)}\Bigg(\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}}P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(y)+\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}e^{-t(n-1)(n+\alpha+\beta+2)}\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(y)\Bigg).

We now give an upper bound on
|E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)|
that is independent of
x,y\in[-1,1]
,
holds for
t\geq T
(where
T=2\log 2\approx 1.386294
is a preselected threshold), and vanishes exponentially as
t\to\infty
.
This result of course implies precise sharp bounds for the Jacobi heat kernel provided that
t
is sufficiently large.
It also implies Theorem
3
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\alpha,\beta>-1
and
x,y\in[-1,1]
. Assume that
t\geq 2\log 2
. Then

\big|E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)\big|\leq\frac{2}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}e^{-(t-1)(\alpha+\beta+2)}\quad\textrm{when}\quad\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2

and

\big|E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)\big|\leq\frac{27/10}{(\alpha+1)(\beta+1)}e^{-(t-1)(\alpha+\beta+2)}\quad\textrm{when}\quad\alpha\vee\beta<-1/2.

We point out the following simple consequence of Theorem
5.2
.

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
\alpha,\beta>-1
and
x,y\in[-1,1]
. Then

(5.3)

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\simeq\left\{{3/2\atop 1/2}\right\}\frac{1}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},\qquad t\geq T_{\alpha,\beta}\vee 2\log 2,

where

T_{\alpha,\beta}=1+\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta+2}\log\bigg(\frac{4}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\bigg)\quad\textrm{when}\quad\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2

and

T_{\alpha,\beta}=1+\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta+2}\log\bigg(\frac{27/5}{(\alpha+1)(\beta+1)}\bigg)\quad\textrm{when}\quad\alpha\vee\beta<-1/2.

Notice that if
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
then
T_{\alpha,\beta}\leq 1+\log 8
, so in this case the bounds (
5.3
) hold in particular
for
t\geq 1+\log 8\approx 3.079442
.

Proving Theorem
5.2
requires some preparations.
To begin with, note that (cf.
[
38
, Chap. IV, Sec. 4.5, (4.5.1)]
)

P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)=\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+\beta+2)x+\frac{1}{2}(\alpha-\beta),

so

\max_{x\in[-1,1]}|P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|=\max\big\{P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(1),-P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(-1)\big\}=\alpha\vee\beta+1.

Thus, for
x,y\in[-1,1]
,

(5.4)

\bigg|\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}}P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)P_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}(y)\bigg|\leq\frac{(\alpha+\beta+3)(\alpha\vee\beta+1)^{2}}{(\alpha+1)(\beta+1)}=\frac{(\alpha+\beta+3)(\alpha\vee\beta+1)}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}.

For general
n\geq 0
we have (cf.
[
38
, Chap. VII, Sec. 7.32, Thm. 7.32.1]
)

(5.5)

\max_{x\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|=\max\big\{P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(1),|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(-1)|\big\}=\binom{n+\alpha\vee\beta}{n}\quad\textrm{when}\;\;\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2.

In case
\alpha\vee\beta<-1/2
, there seems to be no explicit formula known for the maximum, but it is known that its order
of magnitude is
\sim n^{-1/2}
.
The corresponding quantitative bound in terms of
\alpha
and
\beta
does not seem to be available in the literature.
Since the range
\alpha,\beta<-1/2
is not crucial for applications, we afford non-optimal (in the sense of the dependence on
n
)
but relatively simple estimates, which are stated in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,-1/2)
. Then

\max_{x\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|\leq\frac{2n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\binom{n+\alpha\wedge\beta}{n},\qquad n\geq 0.

Proof.

The case
n=0
is trivial, so assume
n\geq 1
.
Using the identity
21
21

21

This identity can be justified, for instance, by combining
[
30
, Chap. 18,
\lx@sectionsign
18.9, Form. 18.9.5]

with the well-known formula
P_{n}^{\beta,\alpha}(-x)=(-1)^{n}P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)
, see
[
30
, Chap. 18,
\lx@sectionsign
18.6(i)]
.

(2n+\alpha+\beta+1)P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}=(n+\alpha+\beta+1)P_{n}^{\alpha+1,\beta}-(n+\beta)P_{n-1}^{\alpha+1,\beta}

we can write

|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|\leq\frac{n+\alpha+\beta+1}{2n+\alpha+\beta+1}|P_{n}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(x)|+\frac{n+\beta}{2n+\alpha+\beta+1}|P_{n-1}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(x)|\leq|P_{n}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(x)|+|P_{n-1}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(x)|.

Combining this with (
5.5
) gives

\max_{x\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|\leq\binom{n+\alpha+1}{n}+\binom{n-1+\alpha+1}{n-1}.

For symmetry reasons, we also have

\max_{x\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|\leq\binom{n+\beta+1}{n}+\binom{n-1+\beta+1}{n-1}.

Now we can choose the tighter of these two bounds, see (
2.13
), which leads to

\max_{x\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|\leq\binom{n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1}{n}+\binom{n-1+\alpha\wedge\beta+1}{n-1}=\frac{2n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\binom{n+\alpha\wedge\beta}{n}.

This is the bound claimed in the lemma.
∎

Next, we estimate for
n\geq 2
the quantity

K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}:=\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}\bigg(\max_{x\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}(x)|\bigg)^{2}.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\alpha,\beta>-1
and
n\geq 2
. Then

K^{\alpha,\beta}_{n}\leq\frac{7\sqrt{6}\,e^{1/30}\,\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}{3\sqrt{5}\,\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+1)}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}\quad\textrm{when}\quad\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2

and

K^{\alpha,\beta}_{n}\leq\frac{18\Gamma(\alpha+1)\Gamma(\beta+1)}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\quad\textrm{when}\quad\alpha\vee\beta<-1/2.

In the proof we shall apply (
2.11
), which together with the bound
1+A\leq e^{A}
,
A\in\mathbb{R}
, implies

(5.6)

\frac{\Gamma(x)}{\Gamma(y)}<e^{1/(12x)}\sqrt{\frac{y}{x}}\Big(1+\frac{x-y}{y}\Big)^{y}e^{-(x-y)}x^{x-y}\leq e^{1/(12x)}\sqrt{\frac{y}{x}}x^{x-y},\qquad x,y>0.

For
x>y
we shall sometimes use (
2.12
) instead.

Proof of Lemma
5.5
.

Throughout the proof we always assume
n\geq 2
.

Consider first the case
\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2
. By (
5.5
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}\binom{n+\alpha\vee\beta}{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}(2n+\alpha+\beta+1)\Gamma(n+\alpha+\beta+1)\Gamma(n+1)}{\Gamma(n+\alpha+1)\Gamma(n+\beta+1)}\bigg(\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha\vee\beta+1)}{\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+1)}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7\cdot 2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}}{3\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+1)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha\vee\beta+1)}{\Gamma(n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha+\beta+2)}{\Gamma(n+1)},

where the inequality emerges from estimating the factor
(2n+\alpha+\beta+1)
by
7(n+\alpha+\beta+1)/3
.
Using (
2.12
) to the second factor above and (
5.6
) to the third one, we further estimate

\displaystyle\frac{K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7\cdot 2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}}{3\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+1)^{2}}(n+\alpha\vee\beta+1)^{|\alpha-\beta|}\,\frac{\sqrt{n+1}\,e^{1/[12(n+\alpha+\beta+2)]}}{\sqrt{n+\alpha+\beta+2}}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{\alpha+\beta+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7\sqrt{6}\,e^{1/30}\,2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}}{3\sqrt{5}\,\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+1)^{2}}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1},

where we applied the relations
|\alpha-\beta|=\alpha\vee\beta-\alpha\wedge\beta
,
\alpha+\beta+|\alpha-\beta|=2(\alpha\vee\beta)
and

n+\alpha\vee\beta+1\leq n+\alpha+\beta+2
. Thus,

K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}\leq\frac{7\sqrt{6}\,e^{1/30}\,\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}{3\sqrt{5}\,\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+1)}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}.

Next, we move to the case
\alpha\vee\beta<-1/2
, i.e.
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,-1/2)
. By Lemma
5.4

\frac{K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}\leq\frac{1}{h_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}\bigg(\frac{2n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\bigg)^{2}\binom{n+\alpha\wedge\beta}{n}^{2}\\
=\frac{(2n+\alpha+\beta+1)\Gamma(n+\alpha+\beta+1)\Gamma(n+1)}{2^{\alpha+\beta+1}\Gamma(n+\alpha+1)\Gamma(n+\beta+1)}\bigg(\frac{2n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}{\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}\bigg)^{2}.

Here we use the bound
2n+\alpha+\beta+1\leq 3(n+\alpha+\beta+1)
getting

\displaystyle\frac{K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\cdot 2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}}{\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha+\beta+2)}{\Gamma(n+1)}\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)^{2}}{\Gamma(n+\alpha+1)\Gamma(n+\beta+1)}(2n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{3\cdot 2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}}{\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha+\beta+2)}{\Gamma(n+\alpha\vee\beta+1)}\frac{\Gamma(n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}{\Gamma(n)}\frac{2n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1}{n}(2n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1).

In the above expression we bound the last two factors by
9/4
and
2(n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)
, respectively.
For two other factors, we apply (
2.12
). This produces

\displaystyle\frac{K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{27\cdot 2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}}{2\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)^{2}}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{\alpha+\beta-\alpha\vee\beta+1}(n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)^{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}(n+\alpha\wedge\beta+1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{27\cdot 2^{-\alpha-\beta-1}}{2\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)^{2}}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}.

Since for
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,-1/2)
one has
\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)>0.88
(see
[
30
, Chap. 5, §5.4(iii)]
), we have

27/[2\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+1)^{2}]<18
. Therefore,

K_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}\leq\frac{18\Gamma(\alpha+1)\Gamma(\beta+1)}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3},

as desired.
∎

Another ingredient needed to prove Theorem
5.2
is the following technical result.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\delta\geq 0
,
\zeta\geq\zeta_{0}\geq 0
and
t\geq T>0
be such that
\zeta\leq 2t(\delta+1)
. Then

\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}e^{-tn(n+\delta)}(n+\delta)^{\zeta}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\Big(\sqrt{2\pi}\sqrt{\zeta_{0}+1}\,e^{-\zeta_{0}+1/[12(\zeta_{0}+1)]}+1\Big)e^{-2t(1+\delta)}2^{\zeta}(1+\delta)^{\zeta}.

Proof.

Denote the above series by
S
and let
\delta,\zeta,t
be as in the statement of the lemma.
Notice that the function
x\mapsto e^{-2t(x+\delta)}(x+\delta)^{\zeta}
is decreasing on
[1,\infty)
. Indeed, its derivative equals

-2te^{-2t(x+\delta)}(x+\delta)^{\zeta}\bigg(1-\frac{\zeta}{2t(x+\delta)}\bigg),

which is negative by the assumption
\zeta\leq 2t(\delta+1)
. Hence,

\displaystyle S\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-2t(x+\delta)}(x+\delta)^{\zeta}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=(2t)^{-\zeta-1}\int_{2t(\delta+1)}^{\infty}e^{-x}x^{\zeta}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=(2t)^{-\zeta-1}e^{-2t(\delta+1)}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-x}\big(x+2t(\delta+1)\big)^{\zeta}\,{\rm d}x.

Using the bound
(A+B)^{\zeta}\leq 2^{\zeta}(A^{\zeta}+B^{\zeta})
,
A,B>0
, and (
2.11
), we further obtain

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\leq(2t)^{-\zeta-1}e^{-2t(\delta+1)}2^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-x}\Big(x^{\zeta}+\big[2t(\delta+1)\big]^{\zeta}\Big)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=(2t)^{-\zeta-1}e^{-2t(\delta+1)}2^{\zeta}\Big(\Gamma(\zeta+1)+\big[2t(\delta+1)\big]^{\zeta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2t}e^{-2t(\delta+1)}(\delta+1)^{\zeta}\bigg[\sqrt{2\pi}\bigg(\frac{\zeta+1}{t(\delta+1)}\bigg)^{\zeta}\sqrt{\zeta+1}e^{-\zeta-1+1/[12(\zeta_{0}+1)]}+2^{\zeta}\bigg].

Now observe that the function
[\zeta_{0},\infty)\ni\zeta\mapsto\sqrt{\zeta+1}e^{-\zeta}

attains its maximum at
\zeta=\zeta_{0}
. Thus,

S\leq\frac{1}{2T}e^{-2t(\delta+1)}2^{\zeta}(\delta+1)^{\zeta}\bigg[\sqrt{2\pi}\Big(1+\frac{1}{\zeta}\Big)^{\zeta}\sqrt{\zeta_{0}+1}e^{-\zeta_{0}-1}e^{1/[12(\zeta_{0}+1)]}+1\bigg].

Since
(1+1/\zeta)^{\zeta}\leq e
, the proof is finished.
∎

We shall use Lemma
5.6
in two cases, which we highlight in the following.

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
\alpha,\beta>-1
and let
t\geq 2\log 2
.

(i)

If
\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-tn(n+\alpha+\beta+3)}(n+\alpha+\beta+3)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\bigg(1+\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}e^{1/12}+1}{128\log 2}\bigg)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}.

(ii)

If
\alpha\vee\beta<-1/2
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-tn(n+\alpha+\beta+3)}(n+\alpha+\beta+3)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\bigg(1+\frac{2\sqrt{\pi}e^{-23/24}+1}{32\log 2}\bigg)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}.

Proof.

In the case
\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2
we apply Lemma
5.6
with
\delta=\alpha+\beta+3
,
\zeta=2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1
,
\zeta_{0}=0

and
T=2\log 2
. Notice that the condition
\zeta\leq 2T(\delta+1)
is fulfilled. Thus,

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-tn(n+\alpha+\beta+3)}(n+\alpha+\beta+3)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}\\
\leq e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{4\log 2}\big(\sqrt{2\pi}e^{1/12}+1\big)e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}2^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}\bigg).

Since
t\geq 2\log 2
, we have

e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}2^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}\leq 2^{-2(\alpha\wedge\beta)-7}\leq 2^{-5},

and this together with the previous bound finishes justification of the estimate in (i).

In the remaining case when
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,-1/2)
we apply Lemma
5.6
with

\delta=\alpha+\beta+3
,
\zeta=2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3
,
\zeta_{0}=1
and
T=2\log 2
. Again,
\zeta\leq 2T(\delta+1)
holds. Hence,

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-tn(n+\alpha+\beta+3)}(n+\alpha+\beta+3)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\\
\leq e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\bigg(1+\frac{2\sqrt{\pi}e^{-23/24}+1}{4\log 2}e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}2^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\bigg).

Much as above, due to the constraint on
t
,

e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}2^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\leq 2^{-2(\alpha\vee\beta)-5}\leq 2^{-3}.

This, in view of the preceding estimate, shows (ii) and concludes the proof.
∎

We are now in a position to prove the main result of Section
5
.

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

Consider first the case
\alpha\vee\beta\geq-1/2
. By (
5.4
) and Lemma
5.5
we obtain

\big|E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)\big|\leq e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+2)}\bigg[\frac{(\alpha+\beta+3)(\alpha\vee\beta+1)}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\\
+\frac{7\sqrt{6}\,e^{1/30}\,\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}{3\sqrt{5}\,\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+1)}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}e^{-t(n-1)(n+\alpha+\beta+2)}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}\bigg].

Applying now Corollary
5.7
(i) we get

(5.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)\big|&\leq e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+2)}\frac{\alpha\vee\beta+1}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\\
&\quad\times\bigg(\alpha+\beta+3+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\,\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+2)}e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}\bigg),\end{split}

where

\mathfrak{C}:=\frac{7\sqrt{6}\,e^{1/30}}{3\sqrt{5}}\Big(1+\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}e^{1/12}+1}{128\log 2}\Big)\approx 2.753612.

To proceed, we estimate first a part of the expression in (
5.7
).
Using (
2.11
) we see that

\frac{\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)\,(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+2)}\leq\frac{e^{1/12}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+2}\,\frac{(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)^{\alpha\wedge\beta+3/2}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}}{(\alpha+\beta+2)^{\alpha+\beta+3/2}(\alpha\vee\beta+2)^{\alpha\vee\beta+3/2}}\\
=\frac{e^{1/12}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+2}\bigg(1+\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta+2}\bigg)^{\alpha+\beta+2}\frac{\big(1+\frac{\alpha\wedge\beta+2}{\alpha\vee\beta+2}\big)^{\alpha\vee\beta+2}}{\big(1+\frac{\alpha\vee\beta+2}{\alpha\wedge\beta+2}\big)^{\alpha\wedge\beta+2}}\frac{\sqrt{(\alpha+\beta+2)(\alpha\vee\beta+2)}}{(\alpha+\beta+4)\sqrt{\alpha\wedge\beta+2}}.

The last factor here is bounded by
1
. Further,
notice that since the function
[1,\infty)\ni s\mapsto\frac{(1+1/s)^{s}}{1+s}
is decreasing,
we have the bound

\frac{\big(1+\frac{A}{B}\big)^{B}}{\big(1+\frac{B}{A}\big)^{A}}=\Bigg(\frac{\big(1+\frac{A}{B}\big)^{B/A}}{1+\frac{B}{A}}\Bigg)^{A}\leq 1,\qquad 0<A\leq B<\infty.

Therefore,

\frac{\Gamma(\alpha\wedge\beta+2)(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+1}}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)\Gamma(\alpha\vee\beta+2)}\leq\frac{e^{1/12}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,e^{2(\alpha\vee\beta)+4}.

Combining the last estimate with (
5.7
) we arrive at

\big|E_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\big|\leq\frac{e^{-(t-1)(\alpha+\beta+2)}}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\bigg[(\alpha+\beta+3)^{2}e^{-(\alpha+\beta+2)}\\
+\frac{\mathfrak{C}e^{1/12}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(\alpha\vee\beta+1)e^{-(t-1)(\alpha\vee\beta+1)}e^{-(t+1)(\alpha\wedge\beta+1)}e^{-2t+2}\bigg].

Applying the simple bounds
se^{-s}\leq e^{-1}
and
s^{2}e^{-s}\leq 4e^{-2}
for
s>0
, we further obtain

\displaystyle\big|E_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-(t-1)(\alpha+\beta+2)}}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\bigg(\frac{4}{e}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\,e^{1/12}}{e(t-1)\sqrt{2\pi}}\,e^{-2t+2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-(t-1)(\alpha+\beta+2)}}{\alpha\wedge\beta+1}\bigg(\frac{4}{e}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\,e^{13/12}}{16\sqrt{2\pi}(2\log 2-1)}\bigg),

where we used the assumption
t\geq 2\log 2
.
The constant in the last parentheses is approximately equal to
1.996640
, which is smaller than
2
.
Consequently, the first bound of the theorem follows.

Now we move to the case
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,-1/2)
. Much as above, by (
5.4
) and Lemma
5.5
we get

\big|E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)\big|\leq e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+2)}\frac{1}{(\alpha+1)(\beta+1)}\bigg[(\alpha+\beta+3)(\alpha\vee\beta+1)^{2}\\
+18\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+2)\Gamma(\beta+2)}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}e^{-t(n-1)(n+\alpha+\beta+2)}(n+\alpha+\beta+2)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\bigg].

Here the ratio involving gamma functions satisfies
22
22

22

To justify this bound, let
f_{r}(s)=\Gamma(s+1)/\Gamma(s+r)
, where
r\in(0,1)
.
Then
f_{r}^{\prime}(s)=f_{r}(s)[\psi(s+1)-\psi(s+r)]>0
, the last inequality since the digamma function

\psi(z)=\Gamma^{\prime}(z)/\Gamma(z)
is increasing for
z>0
; see e.g.
[
30
, Chap. 5,
\lx@sectionsign
5.15, Form. 5.15.1]
.
Thus
f_{r}(s)
is increasing for
s>0
. Using this property first with
r=\beta+1
, and then with

r=1/2
, one gets the desired bound.

\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+2)\Gamma(\beta+2)}{\Gamma(\alpha+\beta+2)}\leq\frac{\Gamma(-1/2+2)\Gamma(-1/2+2)}{\Gamma(-1/2-1/2+2)}=\frac{\pi}{4},\qquad\alpha,\beta\in(-1,-1/2).

Using this and Corollary
5.7
(ii) leads to

\big|E^{\alpha,\beta}_{t}(x,y)\big|\leq\frac{e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+2)}}{(\alpha+1)(\beta+1)}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}+\frac{9\pi}{2}\Big(1+\frac{2\sqrt{\pi}\,e^{-23/24}+1}{32\log 2}\Big)\,e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\bigg].

Notice that

e^{-t(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{2(\alpha\wedge\beta)+3}\leq 2^{-2(\alpha+\beta+4)}(\alpha+\beta+4)^{\alpha+\beta+3}\leq\frac{9}{64},

because the maximum of the function
s\mapsto 2^{-2s}s^{s-1}
on
[2,3]
is attained at
s=3
and equals
9/64
.
Since

\frac{1}{2}+\frac{81\pi}{128}\Big(1+\frac{2\sqrt{\pi}e^{-23/24}+1}{32\log 2}\Big)\approx 2.699527<\frac{27}{10},

we conclude the second bound of the theorem.

The proof of Theorem
5.2
is complete.
∎

6.
Applications: totally explicit sharp heat kernel bounds

In this section we apply the results of Section
4
to derive fully explicit sharp small time
bounds for the Jacobi heat kernel and some other related heat kernels that are of interest.
We put emphasis on simplicity of the bounds, hence the results we present are slightly weaker than e.g. Lemma
F
,
but have uncomplicated form.

Analogous fully explicit sharp medium and large time bounds can then be derived in a straightforward manner, taking into account
the discussion and results presented in Section
5
. We leave the details apart for interested readers.

6.1.
Spherical heat kernel

We use the notation introduced in the beginning of Section
3
.
Recall, cf. (
3.3
), that the heat kernel on the unit sphere
S^{d}
of dimension
d\geq 1
is given directly via the function

K_{t}^{d}(\phi)=\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{2\pi^{d/2}}G_{t}^{d/2-1}(\cos\phi,1),\qquad\phi\in[0,\pi],\quad t>0.

Let
T>0
. By Lemma
G
, for
0<t\leq T
we have

G_{t}^{d/2-1}(\cos\phi,1)\simeq\left\{C_{d/2-1,T}^{\textrm{ref}}\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\atop c_{d/2-1,T}^{\textrm{ref}}\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{t^{d/2}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)},

where

\displaystyle c_{d/2-1,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle=c_{d-3/2,T/4}^{A}4^{d-1/2}b_{d/2-1}\frac{h_{0}^{d-3/2}}{h_{0}^{d/2-1}}

\displaystyle=c_{d-3/2,T/4}^{A}\Big(1\wedge 2^{(d-3)/2}\Big)2^{d}\exp\big(-\chi_{\{d\neq 1\}}\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1}\big)\frac{\Gamma(d-1/2)}{\Gamma(d/2)},

\displaystyle C_{d/2-1,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle=C_{d-3/2,T/4}^{A}4^{d}B_{d/2-1}\frac{h_{0}^{d-3/2}}{h_{0}^{d/2-1}}=C_{d-3/2,T/4}^{A}\Big(1\vee 2^{(d-3)/2}\Big)2^{d+\chi_{\{d\geq 2\}}}\frac{\Gamma(d-1/2)}{\Gamma(d/2)}.

This implies

(6.1)

\begin{split}K_{t}^{d}(\phi)&\simeq\left\{C_{d-3/2,T/4}^{A}2^{2d-\chi_{\{d=1\}}}(1\vee 2^{(d-3)/2})\Gamma(d-1/2)\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\atop c_{d-3/2,T/4}^{A}4^{d-1/2}(1\wedge 2^{(d-3)/2})\exp\big(-\chi_{\{d\neq 1\}}\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1}\big)\Gamma(d-1/2)\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\right\}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)}.\end{split}

Concerning the lower bound for
K_{t}^{d}(\phi)
, we get, see Lemma
A
,

(6.2)

K_{t}^{d}(\phi)\geq 2\Big(1\wedge 2^{(d-3)/2}\Big)\exp\big(-\chi_{\{d\neq 1\}}\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1}\big)\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)}.

This estimate holds for all
t>0
.
To write the upper bounds, it will be convenient to split into cases due to the structure of the upper
multiplicative constant in Lemma
A
.

When
d=1
, the sphere is just the one-dimensional torus. The function
K_{t}^{1}(\phi)
coincides with the periodized
Gauss-Weierstrass kernel,

K_{t}^{1}(\phi)=\vartheta_{t}(\phi)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}W_{t}(\phi+2\pi n),

which is relatively straightforward to estimate directly from the series.
23
23

23

For large
t
one uses the oscillating series, see Section
5
,

K_{t}^{1}(\phi)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-tn^{2}}\cos(n\phi)
.

Thus precise explicit bounds in this case are known for a long time, at least as folklore.
Nevertheless, just for the sake of illustration we note that in this case our results (cf. (
6.1
) and Lemma
A
) imply

K_{t}^{1}(\phi)\leq C^{A}_{-1/2,T/4}2\sqrt{\pi}\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi t}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)}=2\Big(1+2e^{-2\pi^{2}/T}\Big)\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi t}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad t\leq T\leq 2\pi^{2}.

Combining this with (
6.2
) we obtain somewhat rough estimates

K_{t}^{1}(\phi)\simeq\left\{2\big(1+2e^{-2\pi^{2}/T}\big)\atop 1\right\}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi t}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad\phi\in[0,\pi],\quad 0<t\leq T\leq 2\pi^{2};

here the lower bound holds in fact for all
t>0
.

Let now
d=2
. By (
6.1
) and Lemma
A
,

\displaystyle K_{t}^{2}(\phi)

\displaystyle\leq C^{A}_{1/2,T/4}4^{2}\Gamma(3/2)\Psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\frac{1}{4\pi t}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)}

\displaystyle=2\pi e^{T/4}\Psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\frac{1}{4\pi t}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad t\leq T\leq 2\pi^{2}.

This, together with (
6.2
), gives

(6.3)

K_{t}^{2}(\phi)\simeq\left\{2\pi e^{T/4}\Psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\atop\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4}\Psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{4\pi t}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad\phi\in[0,\pi],\quad 0<t\leq T\leq 2\pi^{2}.

Note that for e.g.
T=1
the ratio
2\pi e^{T/4}/(\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4})\approx 12.512168
.

Finally, let
d\geq 3
. Using (
6.1
) and Lemma
A
we obtain

K_{t}^{d}(\phi)\leq\begin{cases}2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{2e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{d-1}\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)(4\pi t)^{-d/2}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)}&\textrm{if}\;\;0<t\leq 2/(d-1/2)\\
2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/\sqrt{2})^{d-1}\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)(4\pi t)^{-d/2}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)}&\textrm{if}\;\;0<t\leq 4/(2d-1)^{2}\end{cases}.

Together with (
6.2
), this gives for
d\geq 3
and
\theta\in[0,\pi]

K_{t}^{d}(\phi)\simeq\left\{2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{2e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{d-1}\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\atop 2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1})\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{2}{d-1/2},

and

K_{t}^{d}(\phi)\simeq\left\{2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/\sqrt{2})^{d-1}\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{b}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\atop 2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1})\Psi_{d/2-1}^{\mathfrak{B}}(t,\pi-\phi,\pi)\right\}\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}e^{-\phi^{2}/(4t)},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{1}{(d-1/2)^{2}}.

In view of (
2.6
) one has
\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1})>\exp(-e^{-\gamma})\exp(-e^{-\gamma}/2)^{d-1}
.
Using this and then approximating the relevant numerical quantities
24
24

24

One has
2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\approx 1.595966
,
2\sqrt{2e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}\approx 4.782514
,

2\exp(-e^{-\gamma})\approx 1.140752
,
\exp(-e^{-\gamma}/2)\approx 0.755232
,

2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 6.987667
,
\pi/\sqrt{2}\approx 2.221441
.

, one can bound the constants

2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}<\frac{8}{5},\quad 2\sqrt{2e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}<\frac{24}{5},\quad 2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1})>\frac{11}{10}\cdot\Big(\frac{3}{4}\Big)^{d-1},\quad 2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}<7,\quad\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{2}}<\frac{9}{4},

for
d\geq 3
, and independently the constants’ ratios as

\frac{2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{2e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{d-1}}{2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1})}<\frac{7}{5}\cdot\Big(\frac{19}{3}\Big)^{d-1},\qquad\frac{2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/\sqrt{2})^{d-1}}{2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{d/2-1})}<\frac{31}{5}\cdot 3^{d-1},\qquad d\geq 3.

We believe that the ratios of optimal upper and lower multiplicative constants in our estimates grow
no slower than exponentially in
d
, due to a certain incompatibility between the structure of these bounds and the actual behavior
of the heat kernel.
An analogous remark pertains to the other heat kernels considered in Sections
6.2
and
6.3
below.

6.2.
Heat kernels on compact rank one symmetric spaces

Let
\mathbb{M}
be a compact rank one symmetric space.
Such spaces are completely classified (cf.
[
15
,
39
]
), precisely
they are Euclidean spheres, real, complex and quaternionic projective spaces,
and the Cayley projective plane over octonions. Thus
\mathbb{M}
is a complete connected Riemannian manifold, with no
boundary and strictly positive sectional (and Ricci) curvature.

We denote by
d_{\mathbb{M}}
the Riemannian geodesic distance on
\mathbb{M}
, and by
\vol_{\mathbb{M}}
the
Riemannian volume measure on
\mathbb{M}
. The dimension
d
of
\mathbb{M}
and the antipodal dimension
25
25

25

Given a fixed point in
\mathbb{M}
, the set of points in
\mathbb{M}
whose geodesic distance from it is equal to
the diameter of
\mathbb{M}
forms a submanifold of
\mathbb{M}
, which is called the antipodal manifold.
In case of spheres it consists of only one point, while in the other cases the antipodal manifolds are identified with
compact rank one symmetric spaces of lower dimension; this identification is independent of the initially fixed point.
The antipodal dimension related to
\mathbb{M}
is the dimension of the antipodal manifold.
For references and more details, see e.g.
[
28
, Sec. 4]
.

\tilde{d}

related to
\mathbb{M}
(both over reals) will be indicated by superscripts, i.e.
\mathbb{M}^{d,\tilde{d}}
means
that
\mathbb{M}
has real dimension
d
and the associated antipodal real dimension
\tilde{d}
.

Let
K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)
,
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
,
t>0
, be the heat kernel on
\mathbb{M}
,
that is the integral kernel, with respect to
\vol_{\mathbb{M}}
, of the semigroup generated by the Laplace-Beltrami
operator on
\mathbb{M}
.
It is known, see
[
28
, Sec. 4]
, that for
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}^{d,\tilde{d}}

(6.4)

K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)=\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{\vol_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,G^{\alpha,\beta}_{\kappa^{2}t}\Big(\cos\big[\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)\big],1\Big),\qquad x,y\in\mathbb{M},\quad t>0,

where

\kappa=\frac{\pi}{\diam\mathbb{M}},\qquad\alpha=\frac{d}{2}-1,\qquad\beta=\frac{d-\tilde{d}}{2}-1.

Totally explicit heat kernel bounds for unit Euclidean spheres are given in Section
6.1

(a simple scaling leads to analogous results for spheres of arbitrary radii).
We now treat the remaining compact rank one symmetric spaces.

In what follows, for technical convenience we represent the upper multiplicative constant of Lemma
A
as

C^{A}_{\lambda,T_{A}}=r_{1}\cdot r_{2}^{\lambda+1/2}/\Gamma(\lambda+1)
, where
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
have appropriate values depending on
the case under consideration.

Real projective spaces

Let
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}^{d,\tilde{d}}
be a real projective space.
Here we consider dimensions
d=2,3,4,\ldots
, since for
d=1
the real projective space is identified with a sphere of
dimension
1
i.e. with a torus. The antipodal dimension is
\tilde{d}=d-1
, hence
the parameters
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
6.4
) take the form

\alpha=\frac{d}{2}-1,\qquad\beta=-\frac{1}{2}.

Further, see e.g.
[
35
, Chap. 1]
,

{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\kappa^{d}\,\Gamma(d/2+1/2)}.

From a general theory, see
[
9
, Thm. 5.6.1]

26
26

26

This result applies in fact to all compact rank one symmetric spaces, but it does not provide a sharp global lower bound
unless
\mathbb{M}
is a real projective space (or a one-dimensional torus).

, it follows that

K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)\geq\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad t>0.

Since our approach does not provide a better lower bound
27
27

27

Our approach provides the same lower bound when the dimension is odd, but it gives a worse lower bound if the dimension is even.

,
we now restrict our attention to upper estimates of
K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)
.

Consider first the case of odd
d
, i.e.
d=3,5,7,\ldots
.
In the context of Lemma
G
, see also Lemma
A
, after simplifications we have

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,C_{d/2-1,-1/2,T}^{\textrm{ref}}=2r_{1}(4r_{2})^{(d-1)/2}\,\frac{\kappa^{d}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}.

Then Lemma
G
together with (
6.4
) gives the following bounds valid for odd
d\geq 3
and
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
:

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{(d-1)/2}\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{d\kappa^{2}},

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{(d-1)/2}\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{(d\kappa)^{2}}.

Approximating the relevant numerical quantities
28
28

28

One has
2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\approx 1.595966
,
2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}\approx 3.381748
,

2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 6.987667
.

, one can bound the constants

2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}<\frac{8}{5},\quad 2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}<\frac{17}{5},\quad 2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}<7,

for odd
d\geq 3
, and the upper and lower constants’ ratios as

\frac{2\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{(d-1)/2}}{1}<\frac{8}{5}\cdot\Big(\frac{17}{5}\Big)^{(d-1)/2},\qquad\frac{2\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{(d-1)/2}}{1}<7\cdot\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}\Big)^{(d-1)/2}.

Moreover, for
d=3
the above bounds for
K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

can be improved with the aid of the finer bounds of Lemma
A
when
\lambda=1/2
.
More precisely, when
d=3
, for
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
we get

K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)\leq{\pi}\exp(T/4)\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{3/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq T/\kappa^{2},

where
0<T\leq 2\pi^{2}
is arbitrary and fixed.

Next, consider the case of even
d
, i.e.
d=2,4,6,\ldots
. To proceed, we make an additional assumption
d\geq 4
;
the dimension
d=2
will be treated separately later.
In the context of Lemma
H
, see also Lemma
A
, after simplifications we have

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,C_{d/2-1,T}^{\textrm{ref}}=4r_{1}\big(32r_{2}^{2}\big)^{(d-1)/2}\,\frac{\kappa^{d}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}.

Then Lemma
H
together with (
6.4
)
gives the following bounds valid for even
d\geq 4
and
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
:

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(8e\,\mathfrak{w}^{2}_{1})^{(d-1)/2}\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{8}{(d-1/2)\kappa^{2}},

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi^{2}/2)^{(d-1)/2}\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{(d-1/2)^{2}\kappa^{2}}.

Approximating the relevant numerical quantities
29
29

29

One has

4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\approx 3.191932
,
8e\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}^{2}\approx 22.872438
,

4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 13.975333
,
\pi^{2}/2\approx 4.934802
.

, one can bound the constants

4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}<{16}/5,\quad 8e\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}^{2}<23,\quad 4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}<14,\quad{\pi^{2}}/{2}<5,

and, for even
d\geq 4
, the constants’ ratios as

\frac{4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(8e\,\mathfrak{w}^{2}_{1})^{(d-1)/2}}{1}<\frac{16}{5}\cdot 23^{(d-1)/2},\qquad\frac{4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi^{2}/2)^{(d-1)/2}}{1}<14\cdot 5^{(d-1)/2}.

Finally, when
d=2
we have

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,C_{0,T}^{\textrm{ref}}=8r_{1}(4r_{2})\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4\pi},

hence, using Lemmas
H
and
A
(the latter with the finer upper bound in case
\lambda=1/2
),
we obtain the following bound for
d=2
and
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
:

K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)\leq 4\pi\exp(T/16)\frac{1}{4\pi t}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq T/\kappa^{2},

where
0<T\leq 8\pi^{2}
is arbitrary and fixed.

Complex projective spaces

Let
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}^{d,\tilde{d}}
be a complex projective space.
Here we consider dimensions
d=4,6,8,\ldots
, since for
d=2
the
complex projective space is identified with a sphere of dimension
2
. The antipodal dimension is
\tilde{d}=d-2
, hence
the parameters
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
6.4
) take the form

\alpha=\frac{d}{2}-1,\qquad\beta=0.

Further, see e.g.
[
35
, Chap. 1]
,

{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})=\frac{(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\kappa^{d}\,\Gamma(d/2+1)}.

In the context of Lemma
G
, see also Lemma
A
, after simplifications we have

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,c_{d/2-1,0,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4}\,\frac{\kappa^{d}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}},

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,C_{d/2-1,0,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle=4r_{1}(4r_{2})^{d/2}\,\frac{\kappa^{d}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}.

Then Lemma
G
together with (
6.4
) gives the following bounds valid for
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
:

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4}\,\Psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{B}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad t>0,

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{d/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{b}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{(d+1)\kappa^{2}},

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{d/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{b}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{(d+1)^{2}\kappa^{2}}.

Approximating the relevant numerical quantities
30
30

30

One has
\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4}\approx 0.644794
,

4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\approx 3.191932
,
2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}\approx 3.381748
,

4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 13.975333
.

, one can bound the constants

\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4}>\frac{3}{5},\quad 4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}<\frac{16}{5},\quad 2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}<\frac{17}{5},\quad 4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}<14,

and the upper and lower constants’ ratios as

\frac{4\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{d/2}}{\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4}}<5\cdot\Big(\frac{17}{5}\Big)^{d/2},\qquad\frac{4\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{d/2}}{\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi/4}}<22\cdot\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}\Big)^{d/2}.

Quaternionic projective spaces

Let
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}^{d,\tilde{d}}
be a quaternionic projective space.
Here we consider dimensions
d=8,12,16,\ldots
, since for
d=4
the
quaternionic projective space is identified with a sphere of dimension
4
. The antipodal dimension is
\tilde{d}=d-4
, hence
the parameters
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
6.4
) take the form

\alpha=\frac{d}{2}-1,\qquad\beta=1.

Further, see e.g.
[
35
, Chap. 1]
,

{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})=\frac{(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\kappa^{d}\,\Gamma(d/2+2)}.

In the context of Lemma
G
, see also Lemma
A
, after simplifications we have

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,c_{d/2-1,1,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle=2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{1})\,\frac{\kappa^{d}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}},

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,C_{d/2-1,1,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle=4\sqrt{2}r_{1}(4r_{2})^{d/2+1}\,\frac{\kappa^{d}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}.

Note that
\mathbb{D}_{1}=(3\sqrt{\pi}/4)^{2/3}\approx 1.208994
.

Then Lemma
G
together with (
6.4
) gives the following bounds valid for
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
:

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\geq 2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{1})\,\Psi_{1}^{\mathfrak{B}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad t>0,

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{d/2+1}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Psi_{1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{(d+3)\kappa^{2}},

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{d/2+1}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Psi_{1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{d/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{(d+3)^{2}\kappa^{2}}.

Approximating the relevant numerical quantities
31
31

31

One has
2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{1})\approx 0.596995
,
4\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\approx 4.514073
,

2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}\approx 3.381748
,
4\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 19.764106
.

, one can bound the constants

2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{1})>\frac{1}{2},\quad 4\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}<\frac{23}{5},\quad 2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}<\frac{17}{5},\quad 4\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}<20,

and the upper and lower constants’ ratios as

\frac{4\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{d/2+1}}{2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{1})}<\frac{38}{5}\cdot\Big(\frac{17}{5}\Big)^{d/2+1},\qquad\frac{4\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{d/2+1}}{2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{1})}<34\cdot\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}\Big)^{d/2+1}.

Cayley projective plane over octonions

Let
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}^{16,8}
be the Cayley projective plane.
Here
d=16
and
\tilde{d}=8
, hence the parameters

and

\alpha=7,\qquad\beta=3.

[

, Chap. 1]
,

{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})=\frac{6(4\pi)^{16/2}}{\kappa^{16}\,\Gamma(16/2+4)}.

, see also Lemma

, after simplifications we have

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,c_{7,3,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{{\vol}_{\mathbb{M}}(\mathbb{M})}\,C_{7,3,T}^{\textrm{ref}}

\displaystyle=16\sqrt{2}r_{1}(4r_{2})^{11}\,\frac{\kappa^{16}}{(4\pi)^{8}}.

Note that
\mathbb{D}_{3}=(105\sqrt{\pi}/16)^{2/7}\approx 2.015904
.

Then Lemma

together with (

) gives the following bounds valid for
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
:

\displaystyle K_{t}^{\mathbb{M}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\geq 2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{3})\,\Psi_{3}^{\mathfrak{B}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{16/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad t>0,

\displaystyle\leq 16\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{11}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Psi_{3}^{\mathfrak{b}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{16/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{23\kappa^{2}},

\displaystyle\leq 16\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{11}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Psi_{3}^{\mathfrak{b}}\big(\kappa^{2}t,\pi-\kappa\,d_{\mathbb{M}}(x,y),\pi\big)\,\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{16/2}}\,e^{-d_{\mathbb{M}}^{2}(x,y)/{(4t)}},\qquad 0<t\leq\frac{4}{(23\kappa)^{2}}.

Approximating the relevant numerical quantities

32

One has
2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{3})\approx 0.266400
,
16\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\approx 18.056293
,

2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}\approx 3.381748
,
16\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\approx 79.056423
.

, one can bound the constants

2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{3})>\frac{1}{4},\quad 16\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}<19,\quad 2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}<\frac{17}{5},\quad 16\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}<80,

and the upper and lower constants’ ratios as

\frac{16\sqrt{2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}(2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1})^{11}}{2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{3})}<68\cdot\Big(\frac{17}{5}\Big)^{11},\qquad\frac{16\sqrt{2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/2)^{11}}{2\exp(-\mathbb{D}_{3})}<297\cdot\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}\Big)^{11}.

6.3.
Jacobi heat kernel

In this section we obtain reasonably precise estimates for the constants appearing in the bounds for

G_{t}^{\alpha,\beta}(\cos\theta,\cos\varphi)
in Lemma
F
.
We carry out the procedure in the most important case
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
.
A similar analysis in the remaining cases requires somewhat more effort, but it is merely a matter of lengthy technical elaboration
which is left to interested readers.

The general scheme of tracing constants via Lemmas
A
–
E
in the unified proof is the following.

\blacktriangleright

In case
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
,

\displaystyle\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}},

\displaystyle\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}},

\displaystyle\Lambda\in(0,\infty)\setminus(\mathbb{N}/2)

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}},

\displaystyle\Lambda\in(-1/2,0)

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:D}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}.

\blacktriangleright

In case
-1<\beta<-1/2\leq\alpha
or
-1<\alpha<-1/2\leq\beta
,

\displaystyle\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad 2\times\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}},

\displaystyle\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad 2\times\big[\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\big],

\displaystyle\Lambda\in(0,\infty)\setminus(\mathbb{N}/2)

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad 2\times\big[\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\big],

\displaystyle\Lambda\in(-1,0)\setminus\{-1/2\}

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\left\langle\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:D}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\atop\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\right\rangle.

\blacktriangleright

In case
-1<\alpha,\beta<-1/2
,

\displaystyle\Lambda\in(-1,-1/2)

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\left\langle\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:D}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\atop 2\times\big[\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\big]\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Lambda=-1

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\left\langle\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:E}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\atop 2\times\big[\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\big]\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Lambda\in(-3/2,-1)

\displaystyle\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\left\langle\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:E}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\atop 2\times\big[\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:C}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:B}}\longrightarrow\textrm{Lem.\,\ref{lem:A}}\big]\right\rangle.

From now on we focus on
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
.

Technical preliminaries

Recall that
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}=\alpha+\beta+1/2
.
The following identities are instant consequences of the relevant formulas.

(6.5)

\Gamma(\alpha+1)\Gamma(\beta+1)\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2^{\Lambda+1/2}}\Gamma(\Lambda+1),\qquad\alpha,\beta>-1,

(6.6)

\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha+2}}{h_{0}^{\alpha}}=\frac{(\alpha+2)(\alpha+1)}{(\alpha+5/2)(\alpha+3/2)},\qquad\frac{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}{h_{0}^{\alpha+1,\beta+1}}=\frac{(\alpha+\beta+3)(\alpha+\beta+2)}{4(\alpha+1)(\beta+1)},\qquad\alpha,\beta>-1,

(6.7)

\frac{\omega_{\lambda+1}(T)}{\omega_{\lambda}(T)}=e^{(2\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil)T},\qquad\frac{\Omega_{\lambda+1}(T)}{\Omega_{\lambda}(T)}=e^{(2\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil+1)T},\qquad T,\lambda>0,

(6.8)

\frac{b_{\lambda+1}}{b_{\lambda}}=\frac{\lambda+1}{(1\wedge 2^{\lambda-1/2})}\exp(\mathbb{D}_{\lambda}-\mathbb{D}_{\lambda+1}),\qquad\frac{B_{\lambda+1}}{B_{\lambda}}=\frac{2^{\lambda+1/2}(\lambda+1)}{(1\vee 2^{\lambda-1/2})},\qquad\lambda>-1/2.

Next, we observe that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2

(6.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{c^{A}_{\lambda+1,T}}{c^{A}_{\lambda,T}}&=\frac{1}{4(\lambda+1)},\\
\frac{C^{A}_{\lambda+1,T}}{C^{A}_{\lambda,T}}&=\frac{1}{\lambda+1}\begin{cases}\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}/2&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=1/(2\lambda+4)\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;\lambda\neq-1/2\\
\pi/8&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=1/(2\lambda+4)^{2}\end{cases}=:\frac{1}{\lambda+1}\mathcal{C}.\end{split}

In general, here and in what follows in Section
6.3
,
for the sake of clarity and unified treatment, we do not take into account finer values of
C^{A}_{\lambda,T}

and wider ranges of
t
provided by Lemma
A
in the cases
\lambda=\pm 1/2
. More precise analysis related to those cases
meets no additional difficulties and is left to interested readers.

We now compute analogous quotients for the constants of Lemma
C
.
For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, denote

\breve{\lambda}:=\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-\lambda

and notice that

\lambda\leq\breve{\lambda}<\lambda+1
.
By Lemmas
C
and
B

(6.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{C}_{\lambda,T}&={c}^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+1/2,T/4}\,\frac{4^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+3/2}b_{\breve{\lambda}}}{\big(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}}T\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big)^{\breve{\lambda}+1/2}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+1/2}}{h_{0}^{\lambda,\breve{\lambda}}}\,\omega_{\lambda}(T),\\
C^{C}_{\lambda,T}&={C}^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}-1/2,T/4}\,4^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+1}B_{\breve{\lambda}-1}\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}-1/2}}{h_{0}^{\lambda,\breve{\lambda}-1}}\,\Omega_{\lambda}(T).\end{split}

It is straightforward to see that for any
T,\lambda>0

\displaystyle\frac{c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}}{c^{C}_{\lambda,T}}

\displaystyle=\frac{c^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+5/2,\ T/4}}{c^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+1/2,\ T/4}}\,4^{2}\,\frac{b_{\breve{\lambda}+1}}{b_{\breve{\lambda}}}\,\frac{\big(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}}T\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big)^{\breve{\lambda}+1/2}}{\big(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+1}T\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big)^{\breve{\lambda}+3/2}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+5/2}}{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+1/2}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda,\breve{\lambda}}}{h_{0}^{\lambda+1,\breve{\lambda}+1}}\,\frac{\omega_{\lambda+1}(T)}{\omega_{\lambda}(T)},

\displaystyle\frac{C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}}{C^{C}_{\lambda,T}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+3/2,\ T/4}}{C^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}-1/2,\ T/4}}\,4^{2}\,\frac{B_{\breve{\lambda}}}{B_{\breve{\lambda}-1}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+3/2}}{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}-1/2}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda,\,\breve{\lambda}-1}}{h_{0}^{\lambda+1,\,\breve{\lambda}}}\,\frac{\Omega_{\lambda+1}(T)}{\Omega_{\lambda}(T)}.

Then using (
6.6
)–(
6.9
) together with the fact that
\lambda>0
implies
\breve{\lambda}\geq 1/2
,
we obtain for
\lambda>0

(6.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}}{c^{C}_{\lambda,T}}&=\frac{1}{4(\lambda+1)}\,\exp(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}}-\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+1})\,\frac{\big(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}}T\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big)^{\breve{\lambda}+1/2}}{\big(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+1}T\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big)^{\breve{\lambda}+3/2}}\,e^{(2\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil)T},\\
\frac{C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}}{C^{C}_{\lambda,T}}&=\frac{4\mathcal{C}^{2}}{(\lambda+1)}\,\frac{2^{\breve{\lambda}-1/2}}{(1\vee 2^{\breve{\lambda}-3/2})}\,e^{(2\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil+1)T}.\end{split}

We will also need the following specification of constants in Lemma
D
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
-1<\lambda<0
and let
T>0
be such that
T/4\leq 1/(2\lambda+2\breve{\lambda}+7)
.
Then, with
c^{A}_{\lambda,T}
and
C^{A}_{\lambda,T}
as in (
6.9
),

\displaystyle c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}\wedge\bigg(c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}\,m_{\lambda+2}\,\frac{8(\lambda+2)}{\pi}e^{-(2\lambda+4)T}\bigg)

\displaystyle=c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}\,m_{\lambda+2}\,\frac{8(\lambda+2)}{\pi}e^{-(2\lambda+4)T},

\displaystyle C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}\vee\bigg(C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}\,M_{\lambda+2}\,\frac{16(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,\Big(\frac{16T}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}\bigg)

\displaystyle=C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}\,M_{\lambda+2}\,\frac{16(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,\Big(\frac{16T}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}.

Consequently, under the assumed conditions

\displaystyle c^{D}_{\lambda,T}

\displaystyle=\frac{64(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,e^{-(4\lambda+6)T}c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}\,m_{\lambda+2},

\displaystyle C^{D}_{\lambda,T}

\displaystyle=\frac{64(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}\,M_{\lambda+2}\,\Big(\frac{16T}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}.

Proof.

From (
2.6
) we see that for
\lambda>-1
and
T/4\leq 1/(2\lambda+2\breve{\lambda}+7)

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+1}T\leq\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+2}T\leq\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}.

Combining this with (
6.11
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{c^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}}{c^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}}\,m_{\lambda+2}\,\frac{8(\lambda+2)}{\pi}e^{-(2\lambda+4)T}&=\frac{2m_{\lambda+2}}{\mathfrak{B}\pi^{3}}\,\exp(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+1}-\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+2})\,e^{(-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil-4)T},\\
\frac{C^{C}_{\lambda+2,T}}{C^{C}_{\lambda+1,T}}\,M_{\lambda+2}\,\frac{16(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,\Big(\frac{16T}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}&\geq\frac{64\mathcal{C}^{2}M_{\lambda+2}}{\pi^{2}}\,\frac{2^{\breve{\lambda}+1/2}}{(1\vee 2^{\breve{\lambda}-1/2})}\,e^{(2\lambda-\lceil 2\lambda\rceil+1)T}.\end{split}

Using now the estimates
{2m_{\lambda+2}}/({\mathfrak{B}\pi^{3}})\leq 1
and

{64\mathcal{C}^{2}M_{\lambda+2}}/{\pi^{2}}\geq 1
we get the desired conclusion.
∎

Finally, note that in the circumstances of Proposition
6.1
we have, see (
6.10
),

(6.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{D}_{\lambda,T}&=\frac{64(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,e^{-(4\lambda+6)T}\,c^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+9/2,T/4}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{4^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+11/2}\,b_{\breve{\lambda}+2}}{\big(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+2}T\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big)^{\breve{\lambda}+5/2}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+9/2}}{h_{0}^{\lambda+2,\breve{\lambda}+2}}\,\omega_{\lambda+2}(T)\,m_{\lambda+2}\\
&=\frac{2^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+12}(\lambda+1)}{\pi^{2}}\,e^{-(4\lambda+6)T}\,c^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+9/2,T/4}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{1}{\big(\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+2}T\vee\mathfrak{B}\pi^{2}\big)^{\breve{\lambda}+5/2}}\,\frac{\Gamma(\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+11/2)}{\Gamma(\lambda+3)}\,\omega_{\lambda+2}(T)\,\frac{1-2^{-\lambda-2}}{e}\exp\big(-\mathbb{D}_{\breve{\lambda}+2}\big),\\
C^{D}_{\lambda,T}&=\frac{64(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)}{\pi^{2}}\,C^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+7/2,T/4}\\
&\qquad\times 4^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+5}\,B_{\breve{\lambda}+1}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+7/2}}{h_{0}^{\lambda+2,\breve{\lambda}+1}}\,\Omega_{\lambda+2}(T)\,M_{\lambda+2}\,\Big(\frac{16T}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1}\\
&=\frac{2^{\lambda+2\breve{\lambda}+27/2}(\lambda+1)}{\pi^{2}}\,C^{A}_{\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+7/2,T/4}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{\Gamma(\lambda+\breve{\lambda}+9/2)}{\Gamma(\lambda+3)}\,\Omega_{\lambda+2}(T)\,\frac{1}{(1-1/e)^{2}}\,\Big(\frac{16T}{\pi^{2}}+1\Big)^{\lambda+1},\end{split}

provided that
T/4\leq 1/(2\lambda+2\breve{\lambda}+7)
;
here we used the fact that
\breve{\lambda}\geq-1/2
if
\lambda>-1
.

We are now in a position to estimate the constants
c_{\alpha,\beta,T}
and
C_{\alpha,\beta,T}
as well as their quotient

C_{\alpha,\beta,T}/c_{\alpha,\beta,T}
. We will consider four subcases of the case
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
according to the scheme presented
at the beginning of Section
6.3
.

The case when

\mathbf{\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}-1/2}
.

To begin with, we focus on the simple case
\Lambda=-1/2
which, given the standing assumption
\alpha,\beta\geq-1/2
,
occurs only when
\alpha=\beta=-1/2
. But then we have

G_{t}^{-1/2}(\theta,\varphi)=\vartheta_{t}(\theta-\varphi)+\vartheta_{t}(\theta+\varphi)\leq 2\vartheta_{t}(\theta-\varphi),\qquad\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi],\quad t>0,

and in view of the upper bound for
K_{t}^{1}(\phi)=\vartheta_{t}(\phi)
obtained in Section
6.1
we conclude that

(6.13)

G_{t}^{-1/2}(\theta,\varphi)\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}\big(1+2e^{-2\pi^{2}/T}\big)\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-(\theta-\varphi)^{2}/(4t)},\qquad\theta,\varphi\in[0,\pi],\quad 0<t\leq T\leq 2\pi^{2}.

Thus, in what follows we may assume that
\Lambda\neq-1/2
, i.e.
\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}+1/2
.

In view of Lemma
F
, we have

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta,T}=c^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,{4^{\Lambda+1}b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}},\qquad C_{\alpha,\beta,T}=C^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,{4^{\Lambda+2}B_{\alpha}B_{\beta}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}.

Using (
6.5
) and (
2.6
) we see that

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle=c^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,4^{\Lambda+1}(1\wedge 2^{\alpha-1/2})(1\wedge 2^{\beta-1/2})\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+1)\Gamma(\beta+1)}{\pi}\,e^{-\chi_{\{\alpha\neq-1/2\}}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}-\chi_{\{\beta\neq-1/2\}}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}

\displaystyle=c^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,4^{\Lambda+1}(1\wedge 2^{\alpha-1/2})(1\wedge 2^{\beta-1/2})\frac{\Gamma(\Lambda+1)}{\sqrt{\pi}2^{\Lambda+1/2}}\,e^{-\chi_{\{\alpha\neq-1/2\}}\mathbb{D}_{\alpha}-\chi_{\{\beta\neq-1/2\}}\mathbb{D}_{\beta}}

\displaystyle\geq c^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,2^{\Lambda-1/2}\,\frac{\Gamma(\Lambda+1)}{\sqrt{\pi}}\,\exp\Big(-\frac{\Lambda+5/2}{e^{\gamma}}\Big),

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle=C^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,4^{\Lambda+2}(1\vee 2^{\alpha-1/2})(1\vee 2^{\beta-1/2})\,2^{-\chi_{\{\alpha=-1/2\}}-\chi_{\{\beta=-1/2\}}}\,\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+1)\Gamma(\beta+1)}{\pi}\,\frac{h_{0}^{\Lambda}}{h_{0}^{\alpha,\beta}}

\displaystyle=C^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,4^{\Lambda+2}(1\vee 2^{\alpha-1/2})(1\vee 2^{\beta-1/2})\,2^{-\chi_{\{\alpha=-1/2\}}-\chi_{\{\beta=-1/2\}}}\,\frac{\Gamma(\Lambda+1)}{\sqrt{\pi}2^{\Lambda+1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}\,2^{2\Lambda+4}\,\frac{\Gamma(\Lambda+1)}{\sqrt{\pi}}

and

\frac{C_{\alpha,\beta,T}}{c_{\alpha,\beta,T}}\leq\frac{C^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}}{c^{A}_{\Lambda,T/4}}\,2^{\Lambda+9/2}\,\exp\Big(\frac{\Lambda+5/2}{e^{\gamma}}\Big).

Summarizing, we obtain

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2^{\Lambda+3/2}\sqrt{\pi}}\,\exp\Big(-\frac{\Lambda+5/2}{e^{\gamma}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}\exp(2e^{-\gamma})\,[2\exp(e^{-\gamma})]^{\Lambda+1/2}}\qquad\textrm{for}\;\;T>0,

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,\beta,T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2\Lambda+4}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\times\begin{cases}\mathfrak{w}_{0}(\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}/2)^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=2/(\Lambda+1)\\
\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/8)^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=1/(\Lambda+1)^{2}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}8\pi^{-1/2}\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\,[2\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}]^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=2/(\Lambda+1)\\
8\pi^{-1/2}\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\,[\pi/2]^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=1/(\Lambda+1)^{2}\end{cases},

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\alpha,\beta,T}}{c_{\alpha,\beta,T}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3\Lambda+11/2}\,\exp\Big(\frac{\Lambda+5/2}{e^{\gamma}}\Big)\times\begin{cases}\mathfrak{w}_{0}(\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}/2)^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=2/(\Lambda+1)\\
\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}(\pi/8)^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=1/(\Lambda+1)^{2}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}16\,\mathfrak{w}_{0}\exp(2e^{-\gamma})\,[4\sqrt{e}\,\mathfrak{w}_{1}\exp(e^{-\gamma})]^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=2/(\Lambda+1)\\
16\sqrt[4]{e}\,\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{0}\exp(2e^{-\gamma})\,[\pi\exp(e^{-\gamma})]^{\Lambda+1/2}&\;\;\textrm{if}\;\;T=1/(\Lambda+1)^{2}\end{cases}.

Approximating above the relevant expressions
33
33

One has

2\sqrt{\pi}\exp(2e^{-\gamma})\approx 10.896385
,

2\exp(e^{-\gamma})\approx 3.506459
,

,
